### y1v1 p.14-15 — Opening monologue: equality is a lie (Ch.1)
[scene] direct address to the reader before the story opens; not a character scene
[narrates] (p.14) "I know this is kind of sudden, but, please, it will only take a moment. I want your honest opinion."
[narrates] (p.14) "Are people equal or not?"
[narrates] (p.14) "A proper society will constantly strive for equality. There are those who clamor for men and women to always be considered equal. As a result, we raised the employment rate for women, we made specialized subway cars only for women. Sometimes, women will even argue over the order of names in a family register. The public opinion of people with disabilities has also changed. We’re now told that we should not use the term “disabled people” when referring to them, so as not to discriminate. Nowadays, children are taught that all people are created equal."
[narrates] (p.14) "But is that true? I have my doubts. If men and women have different abilities, then their roles should also differ. People with disabilities are still disabled, no matter what polite euphemisms you use. No matter how you try to avert your eyes, the meaning of the word does not change."
[narrates] (p.14) "So, my answer would be, “No, we are not equal.” To be human is to be unequal. Equality does not exist."
[narrates] (p.14) "Long ago, in a bygone era, a great man said that heaven does not set one man above or below any other. However, he did not necessarily adhere to the idea that everyone is equal. Did you know that there is more to that famous passage? The rest goes like this: Everyone is equal when they are born, so I ask, why do we see differences in position and status? And it continues: Do you or do you not encourage learning to create a difference?"
[narrates] (p.14) "So then, education creates an imbalance. The point’s spelled out there, in the incredibly famous work Gakumon no Sume. Even though this is the year 2015, the modern era, nothing about these teachings has changed. The situation has only grown more complex and fraught."
[narrates] (p.14) "In any case, we are human beings. We are living, thinking creatures."
[narrates] (p.15) "I don’t think it right to simply say that we are unequal and then live our lives based on pure instinct. In other words, though “equality” is a complete lie, we cannot accept inequality, either. Right now, I’m trying to come up with a new answer for humanity’s eternal question."
[narrates] (p.15) "Hey, you there. You, the one reading this book right now. Have you ever given serious thought to the future?"
[narrates] (p.15) "Have you ever considered the purpose of going to high school or to college? Though the future may seem hazy right now, do you think you’ll find a job someday? That’s what I used to think. Back when I finished my compulsory education and became a high school student, I hadn’t really considered the future. I’d only felt joy at being nearly free of obligation. I didn’t consider the incredible influence that school would continue to have in my life, on my future. I didn’t even understand the purpose behind studying language or numbers."
[trait] philosophical, contrarian on equality; frames the self as a detached analyst

### y1v2 p.15–18 — Ch.2 §2.1 (July 1): first class points, delayed deposit
[scene] (p.15) July 1, Class D homeroom, abnormally raucous: the first monthly point deposit since enrollment is expected.
[thinks] (p.15) System recap for the reader: "We used these private points to purchase daily necessities, meals, even electrical appliances. At this school, points are currency. They are very important."
[thinks] (p.15) "If you didn’t have any private points, you were forced to live day-today without spending money. You couldn’t use real currency anywhere on campus."
[status:points] (p.15–16) Class D sat at zero class points since June; every class started with 1000 class points; deposit = class points × 100 paid into each private account on day 1.
[thinks] (p.16) "However, my perspective changed when I bought a point for the midterm test." — private points can change your situation; you "could likely purchase more than just test points."
[says] (p.17) "Are you disappointed because the gap between the classes has widened, Horikita?"
[thinks] (p.18) "The sharp-witted Hirata hadn’t missed a beat. Splendid." (Hirata decoding Horikita's "get something")
[trait] (p.16–19) Reads Chabashira's hedged closing ("If there are still points left, that is.") as deliberate: "There was deeper meaning behind Chabashira-sensei’s words."

### y1v2 p.19–21 — Ch.2 §2.2 (lunch): having friends and still eating alone
[thinks] (p.19) "Meanwhile, I don’t really belong anywhere."
[thinks] (p.19) "However, now I was experiencing a strange phenomenon: I had friends, yet I was still on my own."
[thinks] (p.19) "I’m over here, guys. It’s okay for you to invite me. My glances were filled with selfishness and anticipation. I was flooded by feelings of self-loathing."
[thinks] (p.19) Fear of the low-tier-friend question: "Did they all consider me a low-tier friend? Or was our friendship all in my head?"
[says] (p.20) "You seem completely used to solitude," (to Horikita; meant as sarcasm, taken by her as sincere)
[says] (p.20) "Doesn’t it cost quite a bit and take a lot of effort to make your own lunch?"
[says] (p.21) "Don’t tell me. Not only are you a bright scholar, you’re an accomplished cook as well? That doesn’t seem to fit your personality."
[says] (p.21) "But why did you decide to go through the trouble of making your own lunch?"
[says] (p.21) "Do I already miss riding the big cafeteria-bound wave of students?"
[thinks] (p.20) Kōenji assessment: "He didn’t love solitude, but rather, loved himself and cared little for others. I respected that he didn’t seem bothered at all by being alone."
[thinks] (p.20) Notes the quiet bespectacled classmate (Sakura) making her own bento: "She was always alone, and I’d never seen her talk to anyone."
[trait] (p.21) Sticks to Horikita despite constant insults: "I wished I had a comeback for that, but I couldn’t argue. I just wanted her to cut me a break."

### y1v2 p.22–26 — Ch.2 §2.3 (after school): Horikita, Kushida, and the "hate" chat
[thinks] (p.22) "There was some value in being able to vanish like a ninja into a crowd. If I hung toward the back of some group of friends, I could pretend I was one of them."
[says] (p.23) "I don’t really think so. What about you, Horikita? You were the only person who gave Sudou a helping hand."

[says] (p.24) "That reminds me. You should join in the chat now and again. You’re the only one who hasn’t caught up in a long time." (telling Horikita to catch up on the class group chat; she keeps notifications off and is "not interested")
[says] (p.25) "I wonder. Well, if I had to think of a reason, I guess I got used to attending school, and then I made some friends. Also, Kushida was probably a big influence."
[says] (p.25) "I admit I found it shocking when she said that she hated you, Horikita. But I think it’s only natural that everyone has people they like and people they hate. There’s no point being concerned about it. I mean, you still pretend to get along well with Kushida-san even though she said she hates you, right?"
[says] (p.25) "Hey…" — followed by: "What the heck? It really hurt when she said it straight out like that."
[says] (p.25) "Were you testing me?"

### y1v2 p.27–38 — Ch.2 §2.4 (night): Sudou's brawl, duplicate keys, Kushida's pressure
[status:points] (p.27) Private balance after the midterm point purchase: "I saw that I had 8,320 private points." — "I had nearly bankrupted myself just to buy the point Sudou needed to pass."
[thinks] (p.27) "Converted, the points came to roughly 8700 yen. Although that wasn’t a giant improvement, it was still big bucks." (87 class points expected payout)
[says] (p.27) "Why’re you here? Actually, forget that – how did you even get in?"
[says] (p.27) "I learned this extremely important fact just now," (learning the group cloned his room key)
[says] (p.27) "No, I can’t forget about it. Hand over the key."
[thinks] (p.27–28) "What an illogical argument. Even if you’re ignorant of committing a crime, it’s still a crime. Friendship doesn’t mean automatically allowing people to do whatever they want."
[says] (p.28) "If you need advice or you’re worried about something, why not ask Ike or Yamauchi?"
[says] (p.29) "Did you insult Chabashira-sensei, by any chance?"
[says] (p.30) "Then what? Did you grab her by the collar and threaten to kill her or something?"
[says] (p.30) "You beat them up? So, uh, why did you do that?"
[says] (p.32) "Well, I wonder about that. I don’t think it’s quite that simple."
[says] (p.32) "Well, the school doesn’t trust you, right? It wouldn’t be that odd for someone in your class, like Kushida, for instance, to support you even if you’re lying. After all, they don’t want their points to go down."
[says] (p.32) "Even if the other party is at fault, it’s still highly likely that Sudou will take some of the blame."
[feat] (p.33) Legal-literacy play: "Sudou hit them, but they didn’t hit Sudou. I think that’s a big part of it. In such a case, claiming it as legitimate self-defense is difficult. Had they come at you with a knife or a metal bat, I think things would have been quite different. Self-defense means that you have the right to defend yourself against sudden, dangerous attacks made against your person. So, I don’t think we can really claim that this was self-defense."
[says] (p.33) "I think the school might have offered an extension because they found it difficult to make a judgment at this time."
[says] (p.33) "Whoever reported it first has the advantage. The victim’s testimony can work as evidence."
[thinks] (p.33) His read on Class C's plan, flagged as imagination: "That was what I imagined their plan to be, anyway." (they let themselves be beaten to destroy Sudou's regular spot)
[says] (p.34) "If trying to explain the situation won’t work, then we’ll need to find ironclad proof,"
[says] (p.34) "So there might be an eyewitness?"
[says] (p.35) "No, at this stage it’s probably only being discussed by the school and the people involved."
[says] (p.35) "If those guys in Class C had intended to spread rumors, we probably would’ve heard about it already."
[says] (p.36) "Yeah. It wouldn’t be good if the accused tried anything,"
[says] (p.36) "There’s just not much I can do. I mean, I can only really respond to Sudou’s story. If it were Horikita or Hirata here, they’d probably be able to give expert advice."
[thinks] (p.36) Toward Kushida: "As Kushida looked up at me with uneasy eyes, I had the sudden urge to embrace her. I stretched my back out and tried to shake those thoughts."
[thinks] (p.36) On the duplicate keys: "Wasn’t it fine for Kushida to have a key, anyway? I suppose that in the delusional part of my brain, giving her a key made it feel like she was my girlfriend. Men certainly are devious creatures."
[says] (p.36) "I don’t know if I should be happy about that or not."
[says] (p.37) "I’m trying, more or less. I just haven’t been able to. Like right now, I didn’t have the guts to promise that I’d help."
[says] (p.37) "Kushida, you’re going to help, aren’t you?"
[says] (p.37) "Remember when I said it’d be better to talk with Horikita or Hirata? Well, Sudou hates Hirata, so that makes Horikita the obvious choice."
[says] (p.37) "Don’t know. We’ll have to ask and find out. I don’t think she’ll just quietly stand by and watch as Class D collapses. Probably."
[says] (p.37) "Is it okay if I’m useless?"
[says] (p.38) "It’s risky. Aside from the question of who started the fight, those three reported it to the school. That means they have the upper hand. Also, I just don’t think it would work, since they started it."
[thinks] (p.38) Chapter close: "No matter how much I thought about it, I couldn’t come up with any solutions."
[rel:sudou-ken] (p.36) Sudou came to him first for help — Kushida: "He came to you before Horikita-san, Hirata-kun, or even Ike-kun."
[rel:kushida-kikyou] (p.37) Keeps her double-nature in view while cooperating: "I remembered that Kushida once directly told me that she hated me... But I might get burned if I forgot about it entirely."
[trait] (p.29–33) Under pressure he interrogates facts, then produces a dispassionate procedural analysis (evidence, reporting order, self-defense definition) rather than emotional reassurance.

[says] (p.59) The perpetrator lecture to Kushida: "I understand a little of what Horikita meant. At the very least, Sudou does share some fault in this case. He’s the type who gets angry easily, right? Whenever he’s faced with someone he doesn’t like, he lashes out, and speaks and acts in an aggressive, domineering way. When I heard he was being considered for a regular spot on the basketball team, I was both surprised and impressed. No one disagrees that he’s an incredible player, but if he acts so arrogant and prideful, some people will come to hate him. Those who work incredibly hard for their spot would probably see Sudou as a rather disagreeable person. Then there are the rumors, right? People say that Sudou’s been fighting people since junior high. I haven’t met anyone who knew Sudou at his previous school, but considering how many people are talking about it, there might be something to the rumor."
[says] (p.59) "This was bound to happen eventually. That’s why Horikita said Sudou was a perpetrator."
[says] (p.59) "Yeah. As long as he continues to antagonize those around him, trouble will inevitably follow. Also, if there’s no proof, then people will use his image against him. In other words, they’ll judge him based on their impressions. For example, let’s say there was a murder case. There are two suspects. One of them has committed murder in the past, while the other is a good, upstanding citizen. Based on that information, who would you believe?"
[says] (p.59) "The truth might be different. However, the less information you have upon which to base your judgment, the more you have to rely on whatever meager information you have at hand. That’s what’s happening here. Horikita cannot overlook the fact that Sudou doesn’t recognize his flaws."
[thinks] (p.60) "I didn’t think this was exactly a 'you reap what you sow' situation, though."
[says] (p.60) "I guess so, yeah. By punishing him, she wants him to understand himself better."
[says] (p.60) "You should act according to your own principles, Kushida. I don’t think it’s wrong to want to help Sudou."
[says] (p.60) "However, I think it would be better if we carefully considered whether or not to directly point out Sudou’s problems. If he doesn’t actually take the time to reflect, there’s no point. There are certain realizations you can only come to on your own."
[feat] (p.60) "Kushida arched her back and stretched; I’d changed her thinking." — influence over the class's social engine, exercised quietly and without credit.
[says] (p.61) "If no one has any objections, how about we start by asking around Class B?"
[says] (p.61) "Because that’s the class that would most want a witness."
[says] (p.61) "Between D and C, which class most threatens Class B? Or to put it another way, which class is more likely to threaten B’s place in the ranking?"
[says] (p.61) "We know too little about Class A. I don’t think they’d want to get involved in a troublesome affair that might negatively impact their points. It's also possible that Class A students don’t care, as they feel no connection to what goes on between C and D."
[thinks] (p.61) Caution about B: "If they had a particularly cunning person, he or she might have devised a plan to defeat not only C, but Class D as well."
[says] (p.61) "Stop." (reflexively grabbing Kushida's collar to halt her charge)
[says] (p.62) "It is certainly true that your excellent communication skills are indispensable. However, this isn’t the same as casually strolling into another class and trying to make friends."
[says] (p.62) "Do you know anyone in Class B?" → "Well then, let’s talk to those people first then."
[says] (p.62) "I see. You might be right about that. We should do what you think is best, Kushida." (concedes the one-by-one vs group-ask debate)
[trait] (p.62) "Suddenly, I felt a sensation, like someone was watching me. I turned around." — watched-sense in a one-third-empty classroom; unresolved in-chapter.
[thinks] (p.61) "So cuuute!" reaction: "She was probably being adorable on purpose… Despite thinking that, my heart pounded like crazy."
[says] (p.64) "I wanna go home." (grumbling at the window during the Class B visit)
[says] (p.64) "You’re probably right," (to Ike's anti-club rant — agreeing while internally cataloguing club benefits: "You gained the skills necessary to build relationships with other people, and you also got to experience victory and defeat.")
[thinks] (p.64) "I waited for a few minutes until I received Kushida’s report. I certainly didn’t expect what she told me." — chapter-ending hook into Ch.4.
[rel:kushida-kikyou] (p.54–62) She commands his schedule by asking nicely; he concedes plans to her judgment while supplying the strategy; collar-grab intimacy registers as both protectiveness and fluster.

<!-- walk: y1v2 ch3 → p.65 -->

### y1v2 p.66–74 — Ch.4 §4.0–4.1: the class-change rule, and the mean-teacher read
[thinks] (p.66) On the search's division of labour: "Meanwhile, I was alone, like always. I could talk to Kushida, but felt disadvantaged when it came to a big group. I couldn’t really talk, so I asked Kushida to fill in me later."
[thinks] (p.67) The only witness to Horikita's contempt for the grievance session: "As her neighbor, I was the only one who noticed." — and on her book: "At a glance, I saw she was reading Dostoevsky’s Demons. A good choice."
[rule] (p.68) He is one of two who can authenticate the class-change claim, and he reads the teacher's glance: "Chabashira-sensei shot a quick glance over at Horikita and me. We had tested out that very method ourselves by buying a test point from the school."
[thinks] (p.68) His prior on the teacher, stated as a rule of the school: "Chabashira-sensei sometimes withheld information, but she didn’t lie."
[thinks] (p.71) His verdict on the club-points reveal: "At any rate, I deduced that our homeroom teacher was being extremely and deliberately mean." — followed by an even-handed note on Ike's side: "If someone were to join a club simply for the sake of earning points, he or she probably wouldn’t see great results... On the other hand, someone who joined the club for the sake of points might discover that they possessed a talent for it."
[says] (p.72) "Horikita. Doesn’t this mean there’s some value in saving Sudou?" — the argument he chooses to make when he decides to move her: points, not feeling.
[says] (p.72) "It’s better to have a lot of private points, right? He can supplement his own grade if he fails, and he can save others."
[thinks] (p.72) "Whether class points or private points, it was better to have a lot of them. There were absolutely no negatives to that. Also, we knew very little about how else to earn points at this stage. If our chances improved with Sudou in the class, then that was reason enough to make an effort."
[scene] (p.73) He stops the moment she concedes: "I considered it unnecessary to say more, so I stopped talking. Horikita pondered the issue in silence."
[thinks] (p.74) His self-assessment on the canvassing: "I hung behind Kushida, Ike, and the others, trailing them like a ghost. I was impressed, surprised, and in awe of their natural conversation skills... How could they talk with people they’d never met before and behave like old friends? Monsters."
[thinks] (p.74) On Horikita's refusal of Kushida's invitations, which he watches daily: "my neighbor—who continued to reject Kushida’s invitations, no matter what—sat with a nonchalant expression as she prepared for class."

### y1v2 p.75–82 — Ch.4 §4.1: Horikita names the witness, and he cannot place her either
[scene] (p.75) He notices the game Sudou is playing while the meeting works: "the person of interest, Sudou, was completely engrossed in some kind of digital basketball game on his phone... I think the game was called Generation of Miracles or something, but I still didn’t quite understand."
[thinks] (p.77) "She spoke as if she had already found the hypothetical witness." — and the room's verdict on it: "No one, myself included, really believed her…until we heard her next words."
[limit] (p.77) Horikita's observation beats his own, and he says so plainly: "I hadn’t noticed that at all. I was genuinely impressed by Horikita’s observational prowess."
[says] (p.78) Horikita's jab at him lands by name: "Since you were one of the people who also stares at Kushida-san, it’s not surprising you didn’t notice."
[says] (p.79) His own contribution to identifying Sakura is a memory of a name only: "I probably know her. I feel like I’ve heard her name somewhere before," — "I felt like I almost had something, but not quite."
[thinks] (p.80) On being lumped in with the boys' appraisal of Sakura's body without having said anything: "Even though I hadn’t said anything, I felt like I was being lumped into the same category as Ike and Yamauchi. Kushida’s bitter smile seemed to say, You’re also fixated on boobs, aren’t you? You disgusting pervert. Granted, that was my persecution complex speaking."
[thinks] (p.82) "I could barely match Sakura’s name to a face. I recalled seeing her back when we were making bets. The bet had been about boobs, though. I guess we were birds of a feather after all."
[scene] (p.85) His taxonomy of the class's four kinds of solitude, with himself as a category: "Kushida and Hirata were proactive and able to open up to just about anyone. Horikita didn’t feel the agony of solitude. So what about Sakura? Did she like being alone, like Horikita? Or was she suffering because she didn’t know how to connect with people, like me?"

### y1v2 p.86–96 — Ch.4 §4.2–4.3: the theory of personal space, and the cameras he had already found
[feat] (p.88) He diagnoses Kushida's failure analytically, in the middle of an awkward conversation, by citing a discipline: "Cultural anthropologist Edward Hall further divided the idea of personal space into four parts. One such zone is the so-called “intimate space.”" — and applies it: "However, Sakura had clearly rejected Kushida. No…rather, it looked like she was running away."
[thinks] (p.88) What he notices about Sakura's body that nobody else in the room reports: "Sakura concealed her dominant hand while also avoiding eye contact. Even if she weren’t comfortable meeting someone’s eyes, people generally looked in the direction of those to whom they were speaking."
[thinks] (p.88) His own reading of Kushida's one-to-one manner, held alongside the class's: "Though it was mostly out of formal obligation, Sakura had exchanged numbers with Kushida. Kushida’s behavior in a one on one conversation was different, though. I don’t think Horikita was wrong to feel some discomfort when speaking with Kushida. I’d found something a bit off-putting about it myself."
[says] (p.90–91) "I don’t think self-defense is all that helpful in this case." — AK: "Ah, I’d accidentally said that aloud." The slip is the same conclusion he gave Sudou privately in Ch.2 (p.33), said now without choosing to.
[scene] (p.91) Kushida invades his space to ask for his loyalty and he catalogues his own reaction instead: "Man, Kushida looked super cute up close. Rather than feeling uncomfortable about this invasion of my personal space, I wanted her to come even closer."
[says] (p.91) "Well… Yeah, I am. Why are you asking me that again, though?" (that he is Sudou's ally, asserted without qualification)
[thinks] (p.91) "If even Sakura, the key witness, denied Sudou her help, then we’d made no progress."
[says] (p.94) "There’s a popular expression in this world: Great talents mature late. Sudou could probably become a professional player in the NBA, right? I think there’s a possibility that he’ll make a great contribution to society. The power of youth is infinite." — his own verdict on the performance: "I uttered a catchphrase that sounded about as original as a television commercial."
[thinks] (p.95) His discovery of the classroom cameras and the reason no one else saw them: "Well, ordinary people don’t typically concern themselves with hidden cameras... Or they might have accidentally spotted them."
[scene] (p.95–96) Horikita asks him to walk back and he touches her forehead first: "After hearing that invitation, I reflexively placed my hand against her forehead. It felt nice and cool, but I noticed the softness of her skin." — "It was rare for Horikita to invite me anywhere. With the world going so topsy-turvy, I wondered if it would rain tomorrow."
[rel:hirata-yousuke] (p.83–84) Hirata briefs him on the witness for Horikita-relay reasons, and invites him to hang out with the couple; AK's interior reaction is the volume's most explicit record of wanting to be included: "Hirata smiled, agreeably. If I were a girl, I would have gone from zero to 100 right there" and "Ahh, I said the exact opposite of what I’d been thinking. Damn, curse my terrible mouth."

### y1v2 p.97–107 — Ch.4 §4.4: the special building, and the friend line he chooses to say
[says] (p.97) "Sudou was the first friend that I made here. I want to help him." — said to Horikita at the scene of the fight; nothing in his narration supports it, and she answers it as a contradiction: "you’re as contradictory as ever, since you’re helping out a friend."
[says] (p.97) "I decided to take some initiative by myself, since I’m not very good at interacting with Hirata, Kushida, or large groups of people. Being social’s definitely not my forte. I thought everyone would make me run around the school or the classroom today, so I decided to bolt instead. I like to avoid trouble, remember?" — the cover persona, offered as his own explanation for being at the building.
[says] (p.99) "Of course I haven’t. It’s your job to come up with plans, Horikita. I won’t ask you to save Sudou, but it’d be nice for you to point Class D in the right direction."
[thinks] (p.99) His read of Horikita's candor, and of everyone else's: "most people aren’t suited for loneliness. Therefore, they sometimes behave hypocritically, like saving someone out of feigned altruism in order to make other people adore them. That didn’t seem to be Horikita’s style, though."
[thinks] (p.100) The case in one line, as he states it to himself at the scene: "One party, either Sudou or the Class C students, had summoned the other to the special building. After that, as if by destiny, the two enemies started brawling... That was the whole case." — "Our only proof was Sudou’s word. We were completely in the dark."
[says] (p.102) "You shouldn’t feel obligated to appear as the witness, Sakura. It’d be pointless to force you to testify. But if someone scary is trying to intimidate you or something, please talk to us. I have no idea how much I can help, but I’ll try." — and his note on the recipient: "Sakura stopped, but didn’t turn around."
[thinks] (p.102) "Are you talking about me?" Horikita muttered — "Ignoring the existence of such a scary monster, I decided to let Sakura go."
[says] (p.102) "Then that’s fine. However, if someone else tries to hound you about it, please tell me."
[thinks] (p.102) How much Horikita's certainty is worth: "So far, we’d been operating based purely on Horikita’s dogmatism and prejudices. It was quite possible that Sakura wasn’t the witness, like she said."
[says] (p.104) He volunteers the whole case to a Class B stranger: "I told her that three people from Class C had called Sudou over, and there was a fistfight..." — his stated reason: "since there wasn’t much information to be gained anyway, it may not have been worth keeping quiet."
[thinks] (p.106) His running account of Ichinose's offer: "Obviously, I couldn’t trust Ichinose yet. She was a student from Class B, and she gained nothing by involving herself. If helping others out of sheer goodwill was reflected in class or private points, then I could understand her motivation."
[thinks] (p.106) On who decides: "I glanced over to see what Horikita thought... I didn’t really have the power to decide in the first place; that was Horikita’s job."
[status] (p.107) The information gap is now his explicit grievance against the school: "This was yet another oversight, another instance where Chabashira-sensei had failed to disclose something important... A usual, our teacher didn’t even pretend to give us equal treatment. I felt discriminated against."

### y1v2 p.116–122 — Ch.4 §4.6: walking to school with Class B's representative
[thinks] (p.116) "It seemed I could have normal, relationship-building conversations after all."
[says] (p.116) "I don’t have to leave so early. I usually hang around my room for about 20 minutes." — his answer to her question, "Aren’t you usually a little late?"
[thinks] (p.116) "Was this the so-called popularity phase I was told would occur three times in my life? I hadn’t experienced it yet; it was about time that I entered my first phase."
[thinks] (p.117) His default reading of any gift the school gives: "It was all incredibly suspicious. I really didn’t think our school was going to be so good to be true. They had to be keeping something from us."
[thinks] (p.118) His own price for the coming summer event: "It would probably be worth more than 10 or 20 points. However, it was difficult to imagine that it would change things by 500 or even 1,000 points."
[thinks] (p.118) "Only people who have meaningful friendships can enjoy a vacation to its fullest. There’s nothing quite as uncomfortable as traveling without close friends, especially when you’re traveling with a group. Just imagining it made me feel like throwing up."
[says] (p.118) "I don’t hate it. I don’t think I do, anyway…" — to "Do you hate traveling?"
[says] (p.118) "Just recalled something a little bit traumatic." — his own staging of the line: "My dry laugher echoed through the hot hallway." (translation typo kept as printed).
[thinks] (p.118) The only trip he owns: "I’d never traveled with a friend before. I had gone to New York with my parents a long, long time ago. Not a single millisecond of it had been fun."
[thinks] (p.119) His private sorting of Class D's misplacement, and the first naming of Yukimura: "Horikita, Kouenji, and Yukimura were undoubtedly three people who deserved to be at the top based on their academics alone."
[says] (p.119) "Isn’t that how societal competition works, though? I don’t think there’s anything particularly strange about that,"
[says] (p.120) "Wouldn’t it be wiser not to talk to someone from another class about this?" — "I asked Ichinose, feeling slightly worried."
[thinks] (p.120) What he is actually checking for: "After all, it was possible she could’ve given me a valuable hint, and I’d do something with it."
[says] (p.120) "I’m not smart enough to engage in an exchange of ideas. All I can say is, ‘I’m sorry.’"
[thinks] (p.121) "That…wasn’t in the any of the hundred million books I’d read." — his reaction to her question about confessions; the grammar is the translation's, kept as printed.
[thinks] (p.121) His unspoken defence, written as a line of dialogue inside his own head: "Was she going to call me disgusting, or a virgin, or a shitty moron?"
[says] (p.121) "Did someone confess to you?"
[says] (p.121) "I don’t think there’s much point in looking for evidence or another witness. Doing that would be a waste of time and a headache." — his own dismissal of the case he is still working; speaker fixed by her answer, "But you went to the scene of the crime the other day to investigate, right?"
[thinks] (p.122) "Even though I knew absolutely nothing was going to happen, I was pretty excited. That was what being a man was all about."

### y1v2 p.123–127 — Ch.4 §4.7: he refuses to be the lie in someone else's confession
[says] (p.123) "So, what should I do next?"
[thinks] (p.123) "No way… Did Ichinose plan to confess to me?!" — the misread the section is built on, corrected in the next line by her "I think someone will confess their feelings to me here".
[says] (p.124) "Wouldn’t it be better if I weren’t here?"
[says] (p.124) "I don’t really think I’m the person to ask for this. I don’t have any experience with romantic confessions. There are probably other people in Class B who could help."
[says] (p.124) "But Ichinose, aren’t you used to people confessing their feelings to you?"
[says] (p.124) "I understand that you don’t want to hurt anyone, but won’t it be worse if they find out you lied?"
[says] (p.125) "Honestly, I think it would be much better for you to talk with this person one-on-one. Truthfully."
[says] (p.126) "I’m just a friend." — "I cut Ichinose off before she could finish."
[says] (p.126) "Ichinose. I didn’t think this was something I should say, considering no one’s ever confessed to me before. But I think it was a mistake for you to call me here." — "I spoke honestly, for both their sakes."
[says] (p.126) The lecture on confession: "It’s true that confessing your feelings isn’t easy to do. You spend every day in complete anguish, as you create the experience in your head over and over. And yet, you still can’t confess your feelings. Even when you think the time has come to actually do it, you can’t say the words ‘I like you’. They get stuck in your throat. That’s what I think. Don’t you think you should answer someone when they desperately long to express their feelings? If you make the situation unclear, both of you may regret it later."
[thinks] (p.126) "Trying to prevent someone’s pain was useless. If you turned someone down, their feelings would inevitably be hurt." and then "Even more hurt if the excuse were built on a lie."
[thinks] (p.126) "I left without waiting for Ichinose’s reply."
[says] (p.127) "That depends on you both."
[says] (p.127) "I’m sorry for acting so full of myself back there," — "I said."
[thinks] (p.127) "I wondered how Class B’s Ichinose Honami planned to resolve this difficult situation. I had to admit, I was looking forward to seeing it."
[trait] (p.126–127) The one extended piece of sincere moral advice he gives all volume is about someone else's confession, delivered to a girl who did not ask for it, and followed by walking away before the answer arrives.

### y1v2 p.128–131 — Ch.4 §4.8: the first call she makes to him, and the silence she cannot finish
[scene] (p.128) "Caller ID displayed the name: Kushida Kikyou. I did a double take to make sure of what I saw. Knowing I wouldn’t have the guts to call her back, I rolled my chair across the room, snatched my phone off the stand, and dove onto the bed."
[says] (p.128) "I don’t think you should feel responsible, Kushida. Not in the least. Besides, she’s going to get it fixed, right? Since it’s so important to her, wouldn’t she get it repaired no matter what?"
[thinks] (p.128) He corrects his own answer mid-call: "However, as I spoke I realized it probably wasn’t that simple." and "It was probably similar to how someone might feel about dining alone in a restaurant."
[says] (p.129) "Do you want me to ask if she knows anything about Sudou’s incident?"
[thinks] (p.129) "Kushida had probably chosen me through the process of elimination, as I was the least harmful candidate. If she’d invited Ike or Yamauchi along, they would have had eyes only for Kushida."
[says] (p.129) "Okay, I understand. Let’s go." — "My voice cracked with a little too much excitement."
[thinks] (p.129) "It was proof that she could invade my personal space without causing discomfort. In my mind, I’d firmly recognized her as my friend."
[thinks] (p.130) His real opinion of Horikita's singularity, withheld: "Horikita wasn’t special. She belonged to a unique class of people. I kept quiet, though, afraid that if I badmouthed her she’d find out about it."
[says] (p.130) "When I think of my relationship with Horikita, it’s like there’s a wall between us. Or perhaps I should say that’s the extent of our relationship, if you understand what I’m getting at."
[says] (p.130) "Ah, that reminds me. I wanted to ask you something. Your room is on the ninth floor, right, Kushida?"
[says] (p.131) "Oh, no reason. Just curious."
[thinks] (p.131) "Ah, if only I were a pretty boy with excellent communication skills. I couldn’t help but wish for that."
[says] (p.131) "It’s getting late. Should I hang up now?" — "I asked, unable to bear the silence."
[thinks] (p.131) His self-assessment on the edge of her almost-sentence: "Sorry, Kushida. If I were on the battlefield, I’d be the sniper, the chicken who would stay far away from the fighting. Forgive me."
[note] (p.129–130) One modest reply in this call carries no speaker tag in the translation and is not assigned here: after Kushida credits "you and Hirata" for Class D coming together, an unattributed voice disowns any specialness and is answered, "I think Hirata would definitely say the same thing."
[rel:kushida-kikyou] (p.128–131) She calls him first, late, to ask a favour; he reports her near-confession and does not ask her to finish it: "I lacked the courage to say" and "to her, so I let it go."

### y1v2 p.132–144 — Ch.4 §4.9–4.10: the mall, and the form he fills in himself
[scene] (p.132) "Our back and forth felt like the template for a date."
[thinks] (p.132) "Seeing Kushida in casual attire for the first time was so overwhelming, I couldn’t look away."
[thinks] (p.133) The gathering he was not told about: "Last week? Really glad I came despite that? What the heck was she talking about?" and "This was the first I’d heard about it. I didn’t recall any previous, hidden event."
[thinks] (p.133) "I looked up to the heavens and lamented my worthlessness. It’s not like Ike and the others were bad for not inviting me. I was the bad guy, the one who hadn’t been invited."
[says] (p.133) "Don’t worry about it. Really, I don’t care. Was it fun?"
[says] (p.135) "I think we’ve had really nice weather lately." — "Worried that we were heading into clichéd territory, I steered the conversation in another direction."
[says] (p.135) "Ah. Sorry. I think I might look a little out of place next to you,"
[says] (p.135) "So, you’re saying someone simple like me is a good match for you? I should accept an insult like that?"
[thinks] (p.135) "I felt the quick thrust of a knife. Maybe I’d dug my own grave by setting myself up like that, but it was still a shock."
[says] (p.136) "Perhaps it was love at first sight?"
[says] (p.136) "No, I don’t think you blend in too much. I definitely felt your presence,"
[feat] (p.136) "Had Sakura really been sitting there the entire time? Amazing that we hadn’t noticed her at all. Not a sign of her presence or aura."
[thinks] (p.139) "Because Kushida didn’t show any signs of disliking the conversation, he probably thought he could successfully ask her out. However, I believed she would try her hardest to sidestep the invitation."
[thinks] (p.140) What he sees and neither girl does: "Both Sakura and Kushida were looking down at the form, so they didn’t notice. But the clerk had unsettling eyes. Even men would find it a little creepy."
[says] (p.140) "Can I see that for a second?"
[says] (p.140) "When the repairs are finished, please contact me."
[says] (p.140) "The manufacturer warranty explicitly shows where the item was sold and the date of purchase. Also, I doubt there’d be any legal problems with me putting my information down. It should be perfectly fine if the user’s name is different than the purchaser’s."
[says] (p.140) "Or, is there a reason why she specifically has to enter her information?"
[thinks] (p.141) "If that’s all it takes to please you, then it actually makes me sorry for you."
[says] (p.141) "Well, you’re making it out to be bigger than it was. Honestly, I was only watching out for that rather weird clerk. I guess he gave the impression that he really, really loved girls."
[thinks] (p.142) "Although my imagination ran wild, I couldn’t let it show on my face. I had to remain neutral."
[scene] (p.142) The question about whether Sakura photographs people carries no speaker tag and is not assigned; only her refusal and his report of it are recorded: "It sounded like she didn’t want to go into the details with me."
[says] (p.143) "Hey Professor, do you have a minute?"
[says] (p.143) "Professor, did you buy your laptop computer from the school, with your points?"
[says] (p.143) "I’m looking for something." — "I explained the gist of what I wanted. Although many similar products sat on the displays in front of me, I didn’t know which one to choose."
[thinks] (p.143) Why he phones a classmate instead of the clerk: "It probably would’ve been faster to just ask the store clerk, but I didn’t want to for various reasons."
[thinks] (p.143) "Sotomura’s nickname was Professor, which undoubtedly made it sound like he was rather intelligent. In reality, he was just a huge otaku. He gathered information daily, comprehensively spanning a wide variety of topics, from dating sims to anime and manga."
[says] (p.144) "Today was just a preliminary inspection. I don’t have enough points to buy anything."
[thinks] (p.144) "When entering a subject that I didn’t understand, it was important to find an expert."
[note] (p.143–144) The object he is shopping for is not named in Chapter 4: it is described only as something with "many similar products" on the display, the visit is booked as a "preliminary inspection", and the purchase is deferred for lack of points.

### y1v2 p.145–151 — Ch.4 §4.10–4.11: the wall around Sakura, and the call that goes nowhere
[thinks] (p.145) "Perhaps this was how people became friends, little by little. Even Sakura, who seemed to have hardly interacted with another person ever, was steadily growing closer to Kushida."

[says] (p.149) "Did something happen?"

### y1v2 p.191–200 — Ch.6 §6.1: the hearing he cannot fix

[note] (p.191) His estimate of Sakura is the chapter’s first alarm: "If she was in this psychological state before the meeting had even begun, she might not be able to speak satisfactorily."
[scene] (p.193) The student council room: three Class C students, Sakagami-sensei, the president and Secretary Tachibana; Horikita freezes at the word "student council."
[note] (p.194) His read of the room before a word of evidence: "Considering they were brother and sister, the odds were not in our favor."
[note] (p.195) He reads Class C’s testimony as invention — the veins on Sudō’s forehead make their account "rather obvious" as a lie — and Horikita’s silence as the real emergency: "It was as if her mind was gone."
[says] (p.197) "Get a grip on yourself, Horikita. We’re going to lose at this rate. You have to fight!"

[action] (p.196–197) The provocation is deliberate and public: he accepts that he "might be killed later for doing this" but judged that no lesser stimulus would bring her back.

### y1v2 p.202–206 — Ch.6 §6.1: the performance of giving up
[note] (p.203) His conclusion about Sakagami before the photos: "no matter what response we volleyed back, I was convinced that he’d do anything to block us."
[says] (p.206) "Horikita, are we really out of options?"
[says] (p.206) "I’m not very smart, so I can’t really come up with a solution. I do, however, think that we probably should accept the compromise that you have offered us, Sakagami-sensei,"
[says] (p.206) "We have no definitive proof of Sudou’s innocence. I suppose I should say such evidence just doesn’t exist. If this event had occurred in a classroom or the convenience store, a greater number of students would have been around to see it, and there probably would have been sound evidence. There’s no record of anyone watching this scene unfold. Since this event took place in the special building, where there weren’t any people around, there’s nothing we can do."
[says] (p.206) "I understand why we’re having this discussion. No matter how much we appeal to the contrary, Class C won’t admit that they lied. Sudou won’t admit that he lied, either. We really would just keep going back and forth. Honestly, it’s getting to the point where I would’ve been happier never having had this discussion in the first place. Don’t you agree?"
[feat] (p.206) The surrender speech is a performance aimed at Horikita, not at Sakagami: "I spoke as if we were accepting defeat." He had decided to speak "perhaps out of respect for Sakura’s earlier courage."
[note] (p.206) The move depends on Sakagami taking it literally, which he does: "In other words, as a declaration of defeat."

## y1v3

### y1v3 p.13–23 — Ch.2 §2.0: the cruise, the first-name fever, and the boundary
[scene] (p.13) Deck of the luxury liner on the way to the school's private island; first-years of Class D around him after midterms and finals.
[narrates] (p.13) The chapter is his first-person account: "The endless summer sea. The infinite blue skies. The perfectly clear air."
[thinks] (p.14) Prices the trip honestly: on his own it "would probably have cost me about 100,000 yen, even in the off season."
[action] (p.15) Shoved by a Class A boy ("Hey, you're in the way. Move it, you defects.") and catches the railing; Sudou answers back, Class D is chased from the bow.
[trait] (p.15) Reads the hierarchy and swallows it: "Although the situation was unfair, we didn't need any unnecessary trouble, so it was best to ignore it."
[thinks] (p.16) Notes Hirata's arrival as rescue: "I was saved by the appearance of my hero, Hirata Man."
[thinks] (p.16) On Hirata and Karuizawa cooling off: "They seemed to get along well whenever I saw them talking."
[voice] (p.18) Feeds Sudou's practice by inventing a wrong first name — "Tomiko. Horikita Tomiko." — then corrects it to Suzune; keeps the joke to himself.
[limit] (p.22–23) Suspects Horikita's hiding ("a slight difference—something felt out of place") but drops it: "it would be silly to investigate further. Best just to drop it."
[thinks] (p.23) The chapter's thesis, stated before he understands it: "I had yet to realize that this place was going to be the boundary between heaven and hell."

### y1v3 p.24–46 — Ch.2 §2.1–2.2: the test is announced, and Class D splits three ways
[scene] (p.24) Beach landing; Mashima-sensei announces the deserted-island special test from a white platform.
[action] (p.30–31) Works the rules the way the chapter needs them read: the penalties page "will determine whether you live or die"; the watches are safety kit — "the school had gifted us these watches to keep us safe."
[feat] (p.31) First to ask the question that sets the economy: "is it possible to get by without using any points?" — Chabashira answers that the school "isn't involved in the test process at all."
[thinks] (p.32) Names the test's real shape: "Essentially, it was a contest of restraint."
[action] (p.44) Stands apart from the toilet fight, watching Hirata get pulled apart: "I stood a short distance away, watching the situation unfold."
[rel:horikita-suzune] (p.44–45) Sidles up to her read of the class; she admits she can't see a solution and he agrees — "I think the same. There's too much we don't know."
[rel:hoshinomiya-chie] (p.38) Given the season-opening advice about confessing to a girl before the ocean; wishes she would leave his love life alone.

### y1v3 p.100–104 — Ch.3: the camera in the luggage, the morning watch, Kanzaki's visit

[place:uninhabited-island] (p.100) The island morning: the mesh tent keeps the night breeze, the luggage is stacked outside to save space, and dawn comes on hot.

### y1v3 p.116 — Ch.3: walking back from the Class C shore
[scene] (p.116) On the path inland past the beach, he turns to scan the Class C shore once more while the walk back is still arguing over what Ryūen's spending means.
[says] (p.116) “The test is literally about freedom. Ryuuen’s way of thinking is one way to approach it. It seems like Ibuki and another student rebelled, and because of that Class C will lose twenty points a day. Since he knew he’d be losing that many points every day no matter what, he came up with a drastic strategy.”
[thinks] (p.116) “Since I didn’t know when Ryuuen had decided to spend all of Class C’s points, I could only guess.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.116) “We ought to think of a way to get points back without giving up. Ryuuen is most definitely wrong. I cannot possibly understand him,”
[says] (p.116) “A zero-point strategy, huh? I see. That’s really interesting.”
[note] (p.116) The walk-back exchange is never tagged; her line is confirmed by the narration that follows it, and both of his stand as the remarks she answers.

[says] (p.132) “Are you getting along well with the other girls? I mean, you can’t really do things alone in this test, right?”
[says] (p.132) “That’s not true. You’re making progress, Sakura-chan.”
[says] (p.132) “It is true. Maybe you can’t see it, but you’re definitely making progress little by little.”
[says] (p.132) “It’s okay. You’ll make some friends really soon, Sakura. Then school will become much more fun.”

[says] (p.133) “That reminds me… That guy, from the incident before…it seems like he resigned.”

[says] (p.133) “Besides, even though you usually act nervous, you always had this really confident expression when you were an idol.”
[says] (p.133) “A long time ago? But when you were featured in magazines you probably didn’t take the pictures yourself, right?”

[says] (p.135) “But if I’m in the picture, wouldn’t that spoil the scenery?”
[thinks] (p.135) Watching her frame the shot with thumb and forefingers: “Preserving memories, huh? So that was why Ibuki had a digital camera.”
[note] (p.135) His inference rather than a fact about Ibuki: her reason for carrying a camera at school is applied to the Class C girl.

[says] (p.137) “Sakura, there’s something I want to ask you…”
[says] (p.137) “You…are you okay with bugs?”
[says] (p.137) “Yeah. Bugs. Like grasshoppers and dragonflies, those kinds of things”

### y1v3 p.144–148 — Ch.5: the solo sweep toward Class A
[scene] (p.144) Fourth day: laughter has replaced complaint — corn, fish and fruit have the class ahead — when he borrows a ballpoint pen, takes his folded map and walks out of base camp.
[number] (p.144) About 100 points spent in total, Kōenji's retirement included; at this pace the class ends the test with points left over, and even Yukimura has no complaints.
[inference] (p.144) Breaks the test down 80:20 — eighty percent defensive cooperation, twenty percent offensive scouting — with the smaller share deciding outcomes the more.
[action] (p.144) Reads the balance as meaning Class D now has to attack the other classes, and sets out for Class A's area around the cave.
[place:uninhabited-island] (p.145) The cliff below the cave: a ladder in a blind spot drops to a hut spot with fishing tackle.
[feat] (p.145) Recalls the facilities seen from the ship, finds the ladder anyone would miss at first glance, and marks the hut's approximate position on his map.
[rule] (p.145) The hut spot means fish without borrowing equipment; the device shows Class A occupying with four hours remaining, and the dusty tools show the gear itself has not been used.
[feat] (p.146) Reads the unoccupied tower as deliberate — holding a visible facility risks observation, and Katsuragi is too cautious for bait this sweet and this close.
[says] (p.146) “So, you won’t occupy this spot out of more than a sense of prudence?”
[note] (p.146) The line is untagged; it voices his reading of the empty tower a moment before the ambush and is recorded as his.
[action] (p.146) Two Class A boys spring the trap — one checks the terminal for a fresh claim, one puts a branch to his throat — and the search takes his pen and folded map.
[says] (p.146) “I’m Ayanokouji, from Class D.”
[says] (p.147) “Give it back.”
[says] (p.147) “I can’t say.”
[says] (p.147) “I can’t say. If I did…I may not be able to return to my class.”
[feat] (p.147) Holds the line through a rapid-fire interrogation — three seconds, four seconds of silence — without naming anyone.
[rule] (p.147) The offer on the table — “There’s one more thing I talk to you about. If you tell us the identity of your leader, we’re prepared to offer a generous reward. 100,000 or 200,000 points.” — first-come, first-serve, signed by memorandum and paid after the test.
[says] (p.147) “You’re asking for me to sell out my class for money?”
[says] (p.147) “Sorry, but I don’t believe it. How would you pay someone? We don’t … have any cell phones here, right?”
[says] (p.148) “A memorandum, huh? Let me ask you something for my own reference… Can you tell me how many points I’d get if I did tell you something?”
[says] (p.148) “Could I have someone trustworthy settle things? For example, someone like Katsuragi. Or perhaps Sa—”

[says] (p.148) “I’ve heard rumors that Katsuragi is Class A’s representative.”

### y1v3 p.159–170 — Ch.6: the underwear in Ike's bag
[scene] (p.159) Wakes before dawn to Shinohara rousing the boys: Karuizawa's underwear is missing from the luggage left outside the girls' tent.

[says] (p.163) “Well, if I think calmly, no solid evidence suggests you’re the culprit, Ike.”
[inference] (p.163) The thief would not keep stolen goods in their own bag, so someone planted the underwear to frame Ike.
[rel:ike-kanji] (p.163) Takes Ike's panic at face value and agrees to carry the planted evidence for him.
[says:ike-kanji] (p.163) “Ayanokouji, you believe me, right? That I didn’t steal them?!”
[says] (p.164) “We have no other choice. You have to put it in your pocket.”
[action] (p.164) Takes the balled-up underwear from Ike and stuffs it into his own back pocket.
[says:ike-kanji] (p.164) “Sorry, sorry. My bag got a little dirty, so I cleaned it off.”
[action] (p.165) Sets his bag down for Hirata's inspection and steps away.
[action] (p.166) Stands perfectly still while Hirata pats him down and feels the cloth in his back pocket.
[rel:hirata-yousuke] (p.166) The one boy who never doubts him — and the one who touches the evidence and says nothing.
[says] (p.166) “Hirata. Why didn’t you tell them?”
[says:hirata-yousuke] (p.166) “That was the underwear in your pocket, right?”
[says] (p.167) “Sure, but…is that really all right?”
[says:hirata-yousuke] (p.167) “If it’s all right with you, may I hold onto the underwear?”
[action] (p.167) Hands the evidence to Hirata, who bags it in a vinyl toilet bag.
[says:hirata-yousuke] (p.167) “But we did find out one piece of bad news here. If the underwear came out of Ike-kun’s bag, then there’s a high chance that the culprit is someone in our class.”
[inference] (p.167) The theft happened between eight last night and seven this morning — likely around sunrise with no flashlight — and anyone in the class could have done it.
[says] (p.168) “You want me to find the culprit?”
[says] (p.168) “Well, I’ll do what I can. Just don’t expect too much of me.”
[says:hirata-yousuke] (p.168) “I believe you’re not the culprit, Ayanokouji-kun. That’s why I saved you.”
[says:hirata-yousuke] (p.169) “If you do happen to find the culprit, I want you to tell me first. I definitely do not want you to tell anyone else.”
[says] (p.169) “So, in other words, you’re going to hide the truth?”
[says:hirata-yousuke] (p.169) “Hide it? That’s a poor choice of words, but there’s nothing I can do about it if people take it that way. Even if one of the guys turns out to the be the culprit, I think it’s better that the truth be concealed.”
[says] (p.169) “I understand. I’ll report to you first. Is that good?”
[thinks] (p.169) “Hirata Yousuke. Are you Class D’s hero?”
[note] (p.169) The printed line in Hirata's speech reads “turns out to the be the culprit” — kept as printed.
[inference] (p.169–170) Hirata's contradiction — believe Ayanokōji, then conceal the truth — reads as distrust: the suspect was handed the detective's role as a lifeline and a warning; Hirata himself may be the culprit.

[action] (p.226) Kills his own light and closes on the signal — the page does not let him catch the conversation.
[action] (p.227) Finds Horikita unconscious by a large tree with her key card on the ground, and lifts her into his arms.
[action] (p.227) Reads the excavated soil on her as proof that more than Ibuki found her position.

### y1v3 p.229–235 — Ch.6 §6.10: carrying Horikita
[scene] (p.229) She wakes in his arms under a tree, fever high, and the night turns on the choice between carrying on and retiring.
[says] (p.229) “Are you awake?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.229) “Ayano…kouji-kun?”
[says] (p.229) “You have a high fever. Don’t push yourself.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.229) “I knew it…Ibuki-san stole my card.”
[says] (p.229) “This isn’t a test where you can just hide for twenty-four hours a day, right? No matter what you do, you can be open to attack.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.229) “I could’ve avoided this if I knew how to rely on someone…”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.229) “When I was losing consciousness, I felt like I could hear Ryuuen’s voice… It’s strange, I thought he’d already retired…”
[says] (p.229) “You were losing consciousness. Maybe you had a dream?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.229) “If it was a dream, it was a nightmare…”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.230) “I’m sorry…”
[says] (p.230) “Why are you apologizing to me?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.230) “There’s no one I can apologize to except for you…”
[says] (p.230) “If you think things are bad, then make some reliable friends. Start there first.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.230) “That’s difficult advice… No one would want to be with me.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.230) “It’s unpleasant to be made fun of, though…”
[says] (p.230) “It’s just that you’ve started to sound like you need allies.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.230) “Nobody would say that…”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.230) “I should have understood this a long time ago…”
[says] (p.230) “Don’t talk. You’re sick.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.231) “I’m going to try to get up to Class A using my own abilities. I’ll definitely recover from this failure…”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.231) “I’m prepared to be hated by everyone else… This was all my mistake.”
[says] (p.231) “According to this school’s system, if you fight by yourself, you won’t reach Class A. We need to cooperate with our classmates. It’s unavoidable.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.231) “I can’t accept that. No matter how hard it is, I’m still…alone.”
[says] (p.231) “Ah, shut up already! Stop talking. Right now, you won’t be able to convince anyone.”
[action] (p.231) Embraces her tightly as she talks of her dream.
[says] (p.231) “You can’t bear every responsibility. You’re not that strong, unfortunately.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.231) “So you’re telling me to give up? I have a dream to reach Class A, a dream for my brother to acknowledge me.”
[says] (p.231) “No one said you have to give up.”
[says] (p.231) “If you can’t fight by yourself, it’s better to fight with a partner. I’ll lend you a hand.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.231) “Why? You’re not the kind of person who would say such things…”
[says] (p.231) “Well, why then? I wonder.”
[action] (p.231) Decides against the emergency call — he does not know the wristwatch button, and a helicopter would give them away.
[action] (p.232) Takes the wrong route, slips on the collapsed soil, and curls around her to shield the fall down the slope.
[action] (p.232) Shelters under an overgrown tree with her head in his lap while the fever burns.
[says] (p.233) “It’s already almost six o’clock, Horikita. You might think this will sound harsh, but you should retire. Your body is probably at its limit.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.233) “I can’t do that. We can’t afford to lose thirty points because of me… I was the one who confronted Karuizawa-san and the others about using points, right? It would make me look like such an idiot…”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.233) “Not just that… The keycard was stolen from me, too. You understand what that means?”
[says] (p.233) “Class D will lose another fifty points.”
[rule] (p.233) Losing the leader’s card means fifty more points, and the class would be left with only a few.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.233) “Just leave me here and head back. If you do, I’ll be the only one absent from roll call.”
[says] (p.233) “What are you planning to do?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.233) “By tomorrow morning, I’ll…try to return by myself, somehow. If I can deal with my poor health during roll call, then I’ll do something about retirement.”
[says] (p.233) “Things aren’t that easy. You’re feeling really weak now, and our teacher isn’t kind enough to let you act your way past her. It will be impossible for you to get back to camp on your own.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.233) “Still, there’s nothing else I can do…”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.234) “Yes. That’s the correct decision. Everything was my responsibility.”
[says] (p.234) “Well then, I’ll leave. But if things continue like this, our classmates will blame you.”
[says] (p.234) “Can you really make it back alone by tomorrow morning?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.234) “Yeah… I’ll be fine.”
[says] (p.234) “Horikita. Do you really think that not retiring is the right decision?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.234) “Of course I do. Retiring isn’t an option for me.”
[says] (p.234) “Hey. Why do you think that we’ve been backed into such a corner?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.234) “I failed due to my negligence. That’s all.”
[says] (p.234) “You’re wrong. You’re completely wrong.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.234) “Go… Because I think of you as my friend, please listen to my request…”
[note] (p.234) She catches herself the moment she says it and covers her mouth.
[action] (p.235) Takes her into his arms again when she loses consciousness.
[says] (p.235) “It would have been easier if you’d just retired of your own free will.”
[thinks] (p.235) “I have never thought of you as my friend. I’ve never cared about you as a classmate. In this world, winning is everything. Your methods don’t matter.”
[thinks] (p.235) “I don’t care what I have to sacrifice. As long as I have my victory in the end, I’ll be fine.”
[thinks] (p.235) “You, Hirata—no, all other people are nothing more than tools. I was complicit in what drove you to this. So, don’t blame yourself, Horikita. You were useful to me.”
[note] (p.235) The monologue answers her friend line without ever being spoken to her.

### y1v3 p.237–238 — Ch.6 §6.10: the ship at 7:58
[scene] (p.237) Reaches the ship at the pier with the storm still on, minutes before roll call.
[action] (p.237) Walks the muddy road by the map in his head, picks her up again, and carries her up the ramp.
[action] (p.237) A teacher stops him on the deck — “You’re prohibited from entering here. You’ll be disqualified.” — and he answers the emergency.
[says] (p.237) “This is an emergency. She’s got a high fever and has lost consciousness. Please let her rest right away.”
[action] (p.237) The teacher brings a stretcher and asks — “Is she okay with retiring?” — and he confirms without hesitation.
[says] (p.237) “Without question. However, allow me to confirm one thing. Because it isn’t eight o’clock yet, this shouldn’t have any effect on roll call, correct?”
[action] (p.237) It is 7:58; the teacher answers — “You are certainly right. It is quite close. However, you’re out.”
[says] (p.237) “I understand. Oh, one more thing. I’d like to return this keycard.”
[action] (p.237) Hands over the key card and leaves the ship before the rain lets up.
[rule] (p.238) Class D loses thirty points to Horikita’s retirement and five more to his absence at roll call.
