### y1v3 p.240–245 — Ch.7: the results and the lone Class C
[scene] (p.240) The test ends at noon on August 7; at the rest area Sudou asks how close he and Horikita are, Hirata brings paper cups, and Ryuuen — the only Class C student left — comes over to provoke.
[says:sudou-ken] (p.240) “Ayanokouji. You work pretty well with Horikita, don’t you? How close are you, really?”
[says] (p.240) “There’s nothing between us. We’re just friends. Nothing more, nothing less.”
[note] (p.240) Untagged; it answers Sudou’s question in his own viewpoint, so it is recorded as his.
[says:sudou-ken] (p.240) “Even that makes me jealous, though. I still ain’t her friend.”
[note] (p.240) “But didn’t Horikita acknowledge you a little bit this time?” runs untagged between Sudou’s lines — it could be either Sudou or Ayanokōji speaking — and is left out as a chapter attribution gap.
[says:sudou-ken] (p.240) “I sure hope so. She still hasn’t called me by my first name, after all.”
[says:hirata-yousuke] (p.240) “Good work, both of you. Thank you for everything you did this week. You really saved us.”
[action] (p.240) Hands him and Sudou the two paper cups he was carrying.
[rel:sudou-ken] (p.240) The question that opens the chapter — how close he and Horikita really are.
[says] (p.241) “I should be thanking you. You covered for me, the class loner. Also, you covered for me being late for roll call, and the fact that Horikita retired.”
[says:hirata-yousuke] (p.241) “I couldn’t blame you when I heard the reason. Besides, Horikita-san gave us some really important information.”
[says] (p.241) “Do you believe what she said?”
[note] (p.241) Untagged; it sits between Hirata’s lines about Horikita, so it is recorded as his.
[says:hirata-yousuke] (p.241) “She isn’t the type to speak irresponsibly. That’s why you get along with her, right?”
[trait] (p.241) The narration calls him the one who would protect an ally even if it risked his pure reputation.
[says:hirata-yousuke] (p.241) “I’d be lying if I said there wasn’t any risk, but I had to help Horikitasan.”
[says:hirata-yousuke] (p.241) “That’s a friend.”
[says:sudou-ken] (p.241) “Wait, what did you say? What are you talking about?”
[note] (p.241) “I think you’ll understand pretty soon. Anyway, Class C is really strange… They’re on another level.” and “Why is he…? So Ryuuen-kun didn’t retire?” run untagged between Sudou’s confusion and his own observation of the beach and are left out as chapter attribution gaps.
[action] (p.241) Finds Class C emptied — Ibuki nowhere on the sandy beach — with Ryuuen alone, the only remaining student, cup in hand.
[says:ryuuen-kakeru] (p.241) “Oh, hey there, sycophants. What happened to Suzune?”
[says:ryuuen-kakeru] (p.241) “I know you’ve been chasing Suzune’s ass. We were together before, you know.”
[action] (p.241–242) Drains his cup, then crushes it and throws it at Ayanokōji’s feet.
[says:ryuuen-kakeru] (p.242) “Throw that away for me.”
[says:sudou-ken] (p.242) “What a stupid thing to say. Huh? Pick up your own trash, jerk.”
[says:ryuuen-kakeru] (p.242) “But picking up garbage seems like the perfect task for garbage.”
[says:hirata-yousuke] (p.242) “Calm down, Sudou-kun. Here, I’ll throw it away.”
[says:ryuuen-kakeru] (p.242) “Who are you? More importantly, where’s Suzune? I’ve dreamed of squeezing her bottom.”
[says:sudou-ken] (p.242) “The next time you say something stupid, I’ll kill you,” Sudou growled.
[note] (p.242) “What are you doing?” and “Huh? What’s with you? Getting all excited by yourself, huh?” run untagged around Sudou’s tagged threat and are left out as chapter attribution gaps.
[says:hirata-yousuke] (p.242) “Horikita-san retired yesterday. She’s not here.”
[says:ryuuen-kakeru] (p.242) “Retired? Suzune? She doesn’t seem like that kind of girl.”
[note] (p.242) Untagged; Hirata has just pulled Sudou away and answers the question about Horikita, so it is recorded as him. “I thought you retired, Ryuuen-kun.” and the interrupted reply “That’s¾” are left out as chapter attribution gaps; the broken three-quarter mark is kept as printed.
[action] (p.242) The megaphone click cuts the argument short as Mashima appears on the beach.
[says:mashima-tomonari] (p.243) “It’s fine. We don’t mind if you want to keep relaxing. The test has already concluded. Now we’ve entered the summer vacation part of the trip, so it’s all right if you loosen up.”
[says:mashima-tomonari] (p.243) “Well then, to get straight to the point. I would like to announce the results of the special test.”
[says:mashima-tomonari] (p.243) “We will not accept any questions regarding the results, no exceptions. We would like you to accept the results you have been given, analyze them, and use them to help you for the next test. It is what it is. Don’t wet yourselves over these results. You must accept reality, you know?”
[says:sudou-ken] (p.243) “That’s what we should be saying to you Class C people. You used up all of your points, right? Don’t make us laugh.”
[says:hirata-yousuke] (p.243) “We have 125 points remaining. I think we’ll be quite fine,”
[says:ryuuen-kakeru] (p.243) “Ha. I’m jealous of the nerve of you small fry. How you can be satisfied with that amount of points…”
[says:sudou-ken] (p.244) “So you’re going to expose a class leader?”
[note] (p.244) Untagged; the narration then has Hirata and Ayanokōji holding their faces still while Sudou alone looks shocked, so the question is placed as his.
[says:ryuuen-kakeru] (p.244) “That’s right. I wrote it on the paper, didn’t I? The name of Class D’s leader.”
[says:ryuuen-kakeru] (p.244) “Also, those guys in A and B wrote the same thing, too. Do you know what this means?”
[says:sudou-ken] (p.244) “Hold on a minute. What are you talking about, huh?! I-If you’re telling the truth, then…”
[rule] (p.244) The page’s own reading of the reveal: if the name holds, Class D is hit with penalties and loses 100 points.
[note] (p.244) “It doesn’t really matter whatever you say; Class C is still going to stay at zero points.” runs untagged between Ryuuen’s taunts and is left out as a chapter attribution gap.
[says:mashima-tomonari] (p.244) “Now, we will announce the rankings. The lowest class is¾Class C, with zero points.”
[says:sudou-ken] (p.244) “Bwah ha ha ha! Hey, check it out! You guys have zero points after all!”
[says:ryuuen-kakeru] (p.244) “Zero?”
[says:mashima-tomonari] (p.244) “In third place is Class A, with 120 points. Coming in second place is Class B, with 140 points.”
[says:mashima-tomonari] (p.244) “…has come in first with 225 points. This concludes the announcement.”
[status] (p.244) Class D takes first with 225 points, Class B second with 140, Class A third with 120, Class C last with zero.
[says:katsuragi-kouhei] (p.245) “Something is strange… What does this mean?”
[note] (p.245) The question follows the narration that he muttered it; recorded as his. The Class A cry “What’s the meaning of this, Katsuragi?!” comes from unnamed voices and is left out as a chapter attribution gap.
[says:sudou-ken] (p.245) “Yahoo! We did it! In your face!”
[says:ike-kanji] (p.245) “Hey, hey, hey, what’s going on?! Hey, hey!!”
[says:hirata-yousuke] (p.245) “I’ll explain everything. Well, Ryuuen-kun, if you’ll excuse us.”
[action] (p.245) Walks toward the boat with Ike and Sudou; Sudou raises a middle finger and sticks out his tongue, and Ryuuen can only watch in silence.

### y1v3 p.245–247 — Ch.7: the deck and the apology
[scene] (p.245) On the ship’s deck Kōenji greets them with a drink; Horikita appears still pale, the class gathers, and Karuizawa comes to apologize.
[says:kouenji-rokusuke] (p.245) “Hello, ladies and gentlemen. How was your week on the deserted island?”
[says:ike-kanji] (p.245) “You jerk, Kouenji! We lost thirty points because of you. You know what I’m talking about, don’t you?”
[says:kouenji-rokusuke] (p.245) “Calm down, little boy Ike. I was in poor health, and was resting. I had no other choice.”
[trait] (p.245) Smooth, glossy skin from a week of tanning under a claim of poor health the page calls not the least bit credible.
[back] (p.245) The thirty points Ike howls about are the retirement penalty from the volume’s second chapter.
[says:sudou-ken] (p.245) “S-Suzune. Are you feeling better now?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.246) “I’m not too bad. I can’t say that I’m back to full health. More than anything else, retiring was a big mistake on my part.”
[says:sudou-ken] (p.246) “Don’t worry about it.”
[rel:horikita-suzune] (p.246) Accepts the first name for one line, then withdraws the permission.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.246) “By the way, Sudou-kun. Don’t just go around calling me by my first name without permission. Understand?”
[says:sudou-ken] (p.246) “Y-y-yeah.”
[note] (p.246) “But…what happened? Why was Class D ranked first?” runs untagged before Karuizawa’s tagged line and is left out as a chapter attribution gap.
[says:hirata-yousuke] (p.246) “Well, Karuizawa-san, I think you should talk to Horikita-san first, don’t you agree?”
[says:hirata-yousuke] (p.246) “Horikita-san, do you have a minute?”
[note] (p.246) The line is attributed only to “he”; Hirata has just proposed the talk, so it is recorded as him.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.246) “Yes. There’s something that we’re supposed to talk about. Right?”
[rel:karuizawa-kei] (p.246) Faces her to eat humble pie after the accusation about the points.
[says:karuizawa-kei] (p.246) “I’m sorry.”
[says:karuizawa-kei] (p.246) “Ibuki-san stole my underwear. Ayanokouji-kun told us everything.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.246) “Huh?”
[note] (p.246) The one-word reply follows the narration that she was puzzled; recorded as hers.
[says:karuizawa-kei] (p.247) “Horikita-san, when you noticed that Ibuki-san was the culprit, she tried to run because you questioned her, right? That’s why you ended up collapsing and getting sick…”
[says:karuizawa-kei] (p.247) “I heard about it from Hirata-kun first. He said that you found out the leaders of Class A and C. That’s why we had so many points. So, I’m…sorry for everything I said.”
[rel:horikita-suzune] (p.247) Delivers the class’s reading of the test — Horikita found the leaders — to her face.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.247) “Wait a minute. I… You said I found out the leaders’ identities? But I retired¾”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.247) “Wait. Ayanokouji-kun, what did you¾”
[note] (p.247) Both replies keep the printed three-quarter marks. The classmates’ praise lines — “There’s no need for you to be humble…”, “Horikita-san, you were so incredibly awesome!…”, “When I heard that you’d retired…” — run untagged from unnamed speakers and are left out as chapter attribution gaps; “W-wait a second!” is hers.
[action] (p.247) He withdraws while the crowd buries the key player in congratulations, and heads for his room.

### y1v3 p.248–249 — Ch.7: the Icarus talk
[scene] (p.248) Chabashira intercepts him on the empty side of the ship and confirms, through the Icarus myth, that the man who demanded his expulsion is his father.
[says:chabashira-sae] (p.248) “May I have a word with you?”
[says] (p.248) “I don’t really feel sociable. Is it okay if I refuse, Chabashira-sensei?”
[says:chabashira-sae] (p.248) “If you really don’t want to, I can just start talking here. You don’t mind if we stand outside, do you?”
[says] (p.248) “It’s hot, so please keep it brief.”
[says] (p.248) “Is it safe to say that for the time being, you’re satisfied?”
[says:chabashira-sae] (p.248) “Yes. First, I’d like to say that you did wonderfully. I’m honestly impressed.”
[says] (p.248) “Well, tell me something. Is it really true? Did he demand I be expelled from school?”
[says:chabashira-sae] (p.248) “Do you have any basis for saying that the story is true?”
[says:chabashira-sae] (p.248) “I know quite a lot about you. Might not that be the reason, more than anything else? Other teachers don’t know about your true abilities. But I have no doubts.”
[feat] (p.248) Claims knowledge of his true abilities that the other teachers do not have.
[says:chabashira-sae] (p.248) “I’m sure that you’ve heard of this rather famous myth, the wings of Icarus.”
[says] (p.248) “Why do you bring that up?” I asked.
[says:chabashira-sae] (p.248) “Icarus flew out of the tower where he was imprisoned in order to gain his freedom. However, that wasn’t achieved through one person’s abilities alone. It was because his father, Daedalus, had constructed wings and instructed him to fly. He didn’t fly because of his own intentions. Don’t you think that sounds exactly like your current predicament?”
[says] (p.249) “I can’t understand.”
[says:chabashira-sae] (p.249) “That man—no, your father—said this: ‘Sooner or later, Kiyotaka will gladly chase the means of his expulsion from school.’ You’re welcoming your end, much like how Icarus fell into the sea and died after his wings were burned, because he’d flown too close to the sun.”
[fact] (p.249) The man who demanded his expulsion from the school is named as his father, with the father’s own words quoted.
[says:chabashira-sae] (p.249) “So, what do you plan to do?” she asked.
[says] (p.249) “You should know, Sensei. Icarus won’t heed Daedalus’ warnings.”
[voice] (p.249) Answers her myth with the myth — burned wings and all, he flies on for his freedom.

### y1v3 p.250–258 — Ch.7 §7.1: the whole explanation
[scene] (p.250) Section 7.1 opens with Horikita storming the lounge for the full story; he gives it on one condition — she never investigates him.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.250) “What do these test results mean? What in the world happened?”
[says] (p.250) “You look like you don’t have any idea at all.”
[note] (p.250) Untagged; it answers her question in his own viewpoint, so it is recorded as his.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.250) “I just can’t imagine it. I don’t understand it at all. I have a mountain of questions.”
[says] (p.250) “I’ll tell you everything. However, my one condition is that you stay quiet on this matter. I won’t compromise on this.”
[goal] (p.250) Sets the price of the story: her silence, with no compromise.
[says] (p.250) “What would you like to ask?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.250) “What were you doing during the test? Tell me,” she said.
[says] (p.250) “When the special test was announced, I didn’t focus on anything except for the additional rules. I roughly understood how to manage the 300 points, but you couldn’t manipulate them as an individual.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.250) “But the additional rules were really difficult to understand. If you did things normally, you wouldn’t have been able to identify the leaders. Right?”
[says] (p.250) “Yeah. First of all, I volunteered to join the search for the base camp. Able to move freely, I planned to search out spot locations ahead of everyone else.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.251) “You make it sound simple, but no one should have known the spot locations.”
[says] (p.251) “That’s not true. You didn’t understand because you were ill and holed up inside the ship, but the school already gave us hints about the locations when we were sailing around the island.”
[fact] (p.251) The circling ship — nearly three times a usual cruise’s speed — and the phrase “significant scenery” were the school’s hints about the spot locations; Katsuragi and Kōenji had noticed the same hint.
[says] (p.251) “Then, I reached the cave. I thought it was the most important base.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.251) “The cave was the most important base? Don’t you think that the river and the well would have been more convenient, though?”
[says] (p.251) “The spot itself wasn’t what was important. Its location was.”
[tactic] (p.251) The cave matters because the hut and the tower — two spots — sit near it, a perfect place to exercise control.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.251) “But what advantage is there in going into the cave if you don’t have a keycard?”
[says] (p.251) “Well, I’d intended to explore various things, but in the end I discovered the leader’s identity.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.251) “Well, I suppose that Katsuragi-kun was careless.”
[says] (p.251) “No, that wasn’t it.”
[says] (p.251) “There was that guy, Yahiko, remember? The one following Katsuragi around? He was the leader. I saw Katsuragi and Yahiko at the cave, but I didn’t see the moment they occupied it. After the two of them walked away, I checked if the cave was occupied or not.”
[fact] (p.251) Totsuka Yahiko was Class A’s leader; Katsuragi only displayed the card while the two were at the cave.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.252) “Wouldn’t you have mistaken Katsuragi-kun as the leader?” she asked.
[says] (p.252) “Do you think that the leader would have shown off the card so carelessly in front of people?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.252) “But why? Why bother deliberately holding onto the card, then?”
[says] (p.252) “Because he didn’t have any other choice. As far as I can tell, Katsuragi is a calm and collected man, excessively cautious. There’s no way he didn’t understand the high risk of occupying a spot immediately after finding it. In other words, the person who occupied it was enticed by shortsighted greed.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.252) “That’s…why there was another person.”
[says] (p.252) “Yes.”
[note] (p.252) The explanation then continues as narration: when Katsuragi found the cave he did not intend to occupy it, but held the space down because Yahiko had been careless, wanting insurance by displaying the card so a witness could be misled into taking him for the leader. The sentences are the page’s, not a spoken quotation.
[says] (p.252) “So, aside from their base, Class A held down at least two spots, but we didn’t confirm how many they were occupying by the end of the test. If I correctly guessed the identity of their leader, though, I could invalidate all of their points.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.252) “I’m still not convinced. If he figured out the spot’s location at such an early stage, and if he were acting alongside a lot of other people, shouldn’t he have avoided trouble? Even if he just had someone standing watch by the cave, it should have been sufficient claim of ownership. Why would they occupy it?”
[says] (p.253) “That was probably Class A’s disadvantage.”
[says] (p.253) “Their class appears perfect at first glance, but it actually has a huge schism in it.”
[fact] (p.253) Class A’s internal schism is the reason Katsuragi could not rely on other people — why the methods revealed Class A so easily.
[says] (p.253) “That’s why I excluded Class A at that stage, and turned my attention toward Class C. Katsuragi was easy to understand, but with Ryuuen, there were many unknown variables. To tell you the truth, he was gathering more information than I was. He’d found out the identities of all of the leaders.”
[rel:ryuuen-kakeru] (p.253) Read as the variable with more information than himself — the identities of all the leaders in hand.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.253) “W-wait, he found out the identities of all of the leaders… So not only Class D, but also the leaders for Classes B and A? But that’s strange. We were far from being penalized; we ended up getting first place by a wide margin. How do you intend to explain that?”
[says] (p.253) “This is a little difficult to explain, but the reason I made you retire is the answer.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.253) “Wait, the answer is you made me retire? What in the world did you do?”
[says] (p.253) “Oh, that reminds me. I didn’t return it to the school yet.”
[action] (p.253) Takes a single card out of his pocket and hands it to Horikita; the engraved name reads “Ayanokouji Kiyotaka.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.253) “This is a keycard. Why do you…?!”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.253) “Wait, why does…?”
[fact] (p.253) The leader card in his pocket bears his own name.
[says] (p.253) “The test had to be fair. The rules were fundamentally created to be fair.”
[note] (p.253) He then asks what would happen if a leader retired because of poor health; the sentence breaks across the page and is left unquoted.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.254) “That’s… The leader would be absent. So the rights of exclusive possession would disappear…”
[says] (p.254) “Wrong. In the manual, it says, ‘It is impossible to change the leader without suitable justification.’ Don’t you think that retirement is suitable enough justification?”
[rule] (p.254) The manual’s leader-change clause — retirement is suitable justification; the additional rules were made to be broken when someone is out through poor health or injury, with the base-camp clause as the parallel case.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.254) “So then, you made me…?”
[fact] (p.254) Horikita Suzune retired and he was appointed in her place — the leader the other classes were supposed to guess at the end, there can only be one.
[says] (p.254) “That’s why even though Class C knew you were the leader, we avoided penalties.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.254) “But wait. Ibuki-san stole my card, but what if I’d protected it really thoroughly?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.254) “Did you drop the card on purpose back then? Well, I suppose that Yamauchi-kun’s actions might have provided Ibuki-san the opportunity to hatch a plan to steal the keycard…”
[rel:yamauchi-haruki] (p.254) The mud bribe read back as the opening that let Ibuki take the card.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.254) “Unless I knew what Ibuki-san was aiming for right at the start, I couldn’t have done anything…”
[fact] (p.255) Ibuki was picked up by Class D, but not by chance — the near-coincidence of two people saved by two separate classes dissolved once Kaneda in Class B came out as a spy Ryuuen had sent.
[says] (p.255) “Besides, Ibuki has a habit of looking people in the eye when she’s lying.”
[trait] (p.255) The bigger the lie, the more obvious the habit — she looked him straight in the eye even over the underwear theft.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.255) “Wait, when she lied she would look the person in the eye? Isn’t it usually the opposite?”
[says] (p.255) “Generally speaking, you avoid eye contact if you have a guilty conscience. However, it was the opposite for her. I think that she made eye contact to make the person think that the lie was the truth. She probably didn’t even notice it herself.”
[says] (p.255) “Her goal was probably to find the keycard, but she might have intended to disrupt Class D at the same time.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.255) “But I have to wonder why Ibuki-san specifically stole my keycard. All she needed was to check my name.”
[says] (p.255) “That was likely Ibuki’s intention from the start. However, she ran into unexpected trouble.”
[fact] (p.255) Ibuki carried a digital camera to take a picture of the keycard; a photo would have made the leader’s identity clear to anyone and let her profit.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.255) “To take a picture…with the camera? Why would she go so far?”
[says] (p.255) “If she had a picture, then the leader’s identity would have been clear for anyone to see, right? If she had convincing evidence, she would have profited.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.255) “I don’t quite understand… Did Ryuuen-kun not trust Ibuki-san?”
[says] (p.255) “That wasn’t it.”
[note] (p.255) The rest of the answer — had the discussion stayed inside Class C there would have been no need for the picture or the card theft — breaks across the page and is left unquoted.
[tactic] (p.256) People outside Class C did not trust Ibuki’s word alone — they wanted reliable evidence, which is why the camera and the card theft existed.
[says] (p.256) “From here on out, I don’t have any evidence of what I’m saying. Think of it as my intuition, which I derived from the test results. At the end of the test, Class A had 270 points.”
[inference] (p.256) His own label: intuition from the results, with no evidence — Class A ended the test with 270 points and had not used a single one.
[fact] (p.256) Classes A and C were connected behind the scenes: Class C sacrificed its own points and bought whatever Class A needed, and by taking all of C’s tools Class A spent the week without using any points — Ibuki’s evidence went to someone in Class A.
[says] (p.256) “By the way, I became aware of Class C’s leader after half of the students retired. It was certain that the leader would have remained on the island, right?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.256) “Even so, we weren’t supposed to know who was left.”
[says] (p.256) “No, I was almost one hundred percent sure that Ryuuen was still on the island.”
[fact] (p.256) Ibuki hiding a wireless transceiver in the ground proved someone was left on the island for her to contact; Ryuuen set it on a table unguarded while on vacation — no one else controlled it, and his mistake was trusting no one.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.257) “My god… I don’t even have the words,” Horikita answered, facing the truth.
[fact] (p.257) The test in one line: Class A failed on its internal rift, Class B ran defense-only and paid for believing Kaneda, Class C sent spies and negotiated with A, and Class D avoided damage because he had been appointed the leader.
[rel:ryuuen-kakeru] (p.257) Named the number one enemy — spies for the leaders’ identities plus whatever the Class A negotiations bought.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.257) “I don’t like this. You completely used me, like a pawn.”
[says] (p.257) “Yeah. I can’t deny that. I wouldn’t be surprised if you never want to speak to me ever again.”
[rel:horikita-suzune] (p.257) Accepts the charge without defense; he was aware of what he had done.
[says] (p.257) “Well, I’ll be heading back to my room now. I’m really tired,” I said.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.257) “Wait. We’re not done talking.”
[says] (p.257) “What? I just want to relax in my room, if possible.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.257) “After you explain everything. There are still some things we need to talk about, right?”
[says] (p.257) “Well…like what?”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.257) “The reason why you participated in this special test. Was it to fight alone? I don’t care that you used me this time. I want to know why you put in the effort when you dislike trouble.”
[says] (p.257) “I wonder.”
[rel:horikita-suzune] (p.257) Her real question is his principles, not the test — the explanation so far was the less important part to her.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.257) “I don’t have any room for doubt. I understand your talents now. If you help me, aiming for Class A seems a realistic enough goal. But what are your principles? Why did you do this?”
[goal] (p.257) Names her own goal with him in it: Class A, realistically, if his principles hold.
[says] (p.257) “Because I was touched that you tried to fight all alone when you were ill.”
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.258) “You wouldn’t normally say that kind of thing. It’s easy to spot the lie.”
[says] (p.258) “Well, what I mean is I don’t feel like explaining it.”
[says] (p.258) “I don’t mind helping you get up to Class A. However, I have one condition. Do not investigate me. If you promise not to touch on this subject ever again, I will help you.”
[rel:horikita-suzune] (p.258) The deal is struck: his help toward Class A in exchange for his past staying buried.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.258) “If you don’t want to talk, there’s nothing I can really do about that. If you’ll help, I have no reason to refuse you. I’m not interested in digging things up that are better left buried. After all, you dislike trouble.”
[rel:horikita-suzune] (p.258) Takes her hand without hesitation; the handshake is firm — “I work for me. You do things for yourself.” — and the battle to raise the class from the bottom begins.

## y1v4

### y1v4 p.12–20 — Ch.2 §2.0: the cabin reverie and the Horikita bridge
[scene] (p.12) Three days after the island test, the cruise-ship respite; Ayanokōji’s cabin reverie, Hirata’s interruption, and the deck lunch that follows.
[back] (p.12) The rule he entered school under: “Contact with anyone outside is forbidden until graduation.” Chabashira’s leverage rides on it — she threatened to expel him from this paradise unless he works toward Class A.
[action] (p.13) A “little devil” perched on his shoulder whispers that “It’s better to get them before they get you”; he spends a second imagining forcing Chabashira to resign, then returns to blank thinking.
[says] (p.13) “Sigh. If only I had the power to rotate the earth on its axis myself…”
[trait] (p.13) Rates the boys aboard as “beasts—sex-starved carnivores” waiting like predators while the girls chatter — his anthropology of the vacation mood.
[says] (p.14) “I’ll pass. I don’t think I’m on especially good terms with Karuizawa’s group.”
[trait] (p.14) Grades the friendship tiers: at the bottom strata, “low-level friends are only invited ten percent of the time” — and he is the lowly friend.
[says] (p.15) “Well, I don’t particularly dislike the idea, but… Didn’t you already promise Karuizawa?”
[action] (p.16) Asks after Karuizawa’s recovered underwear and is satisfied with Hirata’s quiet resolution of the fiasco.
[trait] (p.17) Reads Hirata’s invitation as carrying “some kind of ulterior motive” before the ask lands.
[says] (p.18) “But even if I can bring you both together, things won’t be that easy. Horikita is difficult.”
[goal] (p.18) Takes the go-between job while admitting he usually just goes along with whatever Horikita says — “I’ve never really forced my opinion on her.”
[action] (p.20) Executes his “Speedy Escape” the moment Karuizawa’s group closes ranks over Hirata.
[rel:hirata-yousuke] (pp.13–20) Pressed into service as the Horikita bridge over lunch; he takes the ask but not the urgency.
[rel:karuizawa-kei] (pp.14, 20) Declines her group, then watches her reclaim Hirata anyway.
[rel:horikita-suzune] (pp.17–19) The subject of Hirata’s request; he warns she will shut down if she senses manipulation.

### y1v4 p.21–25 — Ch.2 §2.1: the Kōenji corridor and Sakura’s call
[scene] (p.21) Corridor on the third deck: Kōenji dripping from the pool, the bellhop’s fourth warning, and Ayanokōji’s escape route.
[says] (p.23) “Do you want something from me?”
[action] (p.23) Throws the bellhop’s warning back at Kōenji — “I passed the ball, if you will” — to engineer his own escape.
[feat] (p.24) Reads the ship’s signboard map and fixes the layout in his head: nine decks plus a roof, guys on the third deck and girls on the fourth, the 24-hour lounge on the first.
[action] (p.25) Takes Sakura’s email call rather than kill time alone.

[says] (p.27) “You’re worried because even though you want to get along with them, you don’t think you can?”
[says] (p.28) “It’s no big deal. I don’t mind it when you come to me for advice. Whether I’ll be of much help is another story.”

[says] (p.31) “Understood.” (chat)

[rel:karuizawa-kei] (pp.33–36) The fourth member; she moves her chair away from him.

### y1v4 p.37–49 — Ch.2 §2.3: the four outcomes and the traitor rules
[scene] (p.37) Still at Mashima’s table: the group roster, the handout of four outcomes, and the Werewolf detour, all read through Ayanokōji’s eyes.
[says] (p.37) “Karuizawa, if you don’t change your attitude toward the teachers, it might leave a mark on your permanent record. That wouldn’t be good, right?”

[note] (p.39) Knows Ichinose from Class B and Ibuki from Class C off the printed roster; of Ibuki he thinks being in the same group “was a perverse kind of fate.”
[feat] (p.43) Has never heard of the party game Werewolf — Karuizawa’s amazement lands on genuine ignorance.
[thinks] (p.45) Reads the traitor rules as flipping the test on its head: with answers accepted any time, no one would seriously aim for Outcome #1.
[thinks] (pp.47–48) Reduces the exam to four possibilities — the honest Outcome #1, a wrong guess at the end, the traitor finding the VIP, and the traitor misled — and wants to avoid the last one most of all.
[feat] (p.48) Easily memorizes the prohibited-actions list: phone theft, threats for VIP information, impersonation by borrowed phone, and cross-class discussion until the exam ends — each punishable up to expulsion.
[trait] (p.48) Notes the school’s anonymity design means any school-sent message is “100% the truth.”
[rel:karuizawa-kei] (p.37) Warns her about her permanent record; she brushes it off.
[rel:mashima-tomonari] (pp.37–49) The briefer he watches absorb Karuizawa’s interruptions without expression.

### y1v4 p.50–51 — Ch.2 §2.3: the hallway split
[scene] (p.50) Leaving Mashima’s room: Yukimura’s huddle plan collapses when Karuizawa calls Hirata and the Professor declines.
[says] (p.50) “For the most part, I guess. I’d like you to explain the things I don’t fully understand later, though.” (to Yukimura, who asked whether he understood)
[trait] (p.50) Feigns incompleteness on purpose — “I wasn’t going to tell Hirata anything more” in the later hallway talk — and lets Yukimura’s discontent pass unnoticed.

[rel:yukimura-teruhiko] (pp.50–51) The one classmate who tries to build a three-man huddle with him; Yukimura sighs and drops it.

### y1v4 p.52–53 — Ch.2 §2.4: the wake-up and the scattered explanations
[scene] (p.52) Just before 20:30 he wakes in the third-deck cabin to Hirata’s apology, and they step out together toward the evening’s meetings.
[says] (p.53) “Can’t really say. I haven’t been thinking about the exam like you, Horikita, and Yukimura have. I’m not smart, either, so I haven’t really noticed much.” (deflecting Hirata’s request for observations)
[says] (p.53) “Yeah, I think you’re right, Hirata. It would definitely be more efficient to explain the test to all the students at the same time, rather than dividing us into groups and doing it in stages.”
[thinks] (p.53) Suspects the school’s deliberately scattered briefings mean their “thinking” is already being tested.
[rel:hirata-yousuke] (pp.52–53) Walks him to the second deck while sharing nothing of substance.

[scene] (p.54) At the second-deck meeting place he watches Katsuragi confront Horikita, Kanzaki intercede, Ryūen prowl, and Kushida arrive puzzled.
[says] (p.54) “Who are those people?” (asking Hirata to name the waiting students)
[action] (p.54) Burns Morimiya and Tokitō’s names and faces into memory on Hirata’s tip, then keeps to the line without making a fuss.
[thinks] (p.55) Notes Katsuragi’s entourage and Horikita’s changed standing: other classes now see her as “a puppet master, a mastermind who worked behind the scenes.”
[thinks] (p.57) Credits Horikita’s cold-shoulder resilience — the island win is his, but she took the spotlight to keep him hidden.
[thinks] (p.58) Expects a huge showdown between Katsuragi and Ryūen before the test begins; it doesn’t come.
[says] (p.61) “Well then, good luck.” (leaving Hirata at the gathering)

[rel:katsuragi-kouhei] (pp.54–60) Watches the Class A leader pivot from ignoring Horikita to warning her.
[rel:ryuuen-kakeru] (pp.57–60) Notes the demented chuckle and the docile lackeys trailing him.
[rel:kushida-kikyou] (p.60) Present when she wanders into the gathering, puzzled.

[scene] (p.78) Meets Yukimura and the Professor outside the Rabbit room, then sits through the first group discussion as Ichinose and Machida trade Katsuragi’s no-discussion plan.
[says] (p.79) “No, I don’t have a favorite speech pattern or anything. Not especially, anyway,” I answered. (to the Professor’s moe-dialect question)

### y1v4 p.97–102 — Ch.3 §3.2: the bow with Sakura
[scene] (p.98) Meets Sakura behind a pillar on the quiet bow deck; she stress-mutters, hands him the Cow group list, and they compete over who has fewer friends.
[says] (p.98) “Sakura? What’s the matter?” I asked, trying not to surprise her.
[says] (p.100) “I didn’t really hear anything. I just got here now.”
[says] (p.100) “So, why did you call me out here?” I asked.

[says] (p.101) “Sorry. I don’t really have any friends, either,” I said.
[says] (p.101) “By the way, there was something I wanted to ask you, Sakura.”
[says] (p.101) “Has Yamauchi gotten in touch with you at all since the discussion ended?”
[back] (pp.101–102) Island after-action: he used Sakura while using Horikita, and manipulated Yamauchi with a promise of her email he had not yet asked her for — “Since I’d planted the seeds myself, if Yamauchi made a move, I would have to act.”
[says] (p.101) “For the time being, contact me if something’s bothering you. You can tell me anything,” I said.
[says] (p.101) “Yeah. That’s the least I can do.”
[trait] (p.102) Watches her develop quickly since the island — more assertive, eyes sparkling — and notes she has learned to stay positive without transforming completely.

### y1v4 p.103–105 — Ch.3 §3.3: the Yamauchi redirect
[scene] (p.103) Choked by Yamauchi Haruki the moment he re-enters the ship; the Sakura-email lie becomes a camera-and-points pep talk.
[says] (p.103) “Wh-what’s the matter?” I knew the reason, but asked as a formality.
[says] (p.103) “I never meant to go after her. Well, this is kind of hard to say, but…I lied to you before,” I croaked.
[says] (p.103) “Do you really think that a loner like me would know Sakura’s email address?” I turned toward him, trying to show my sincerity.
[says] (p.103) “Yeah. Sorry.”
[says] (p.104) “Can you give me just a little more time?” I asked.
[says] (p.104) “Then are you going to make Sakura tell you?” I asked.
[says] (p.104) “You know, Sakura said she hates guys that are all talk.”
[says] (p.104) “Of course she hates me. But it should be obvious why I couldn’t give you her contact information. I don’t want you to make the same mistake I did, Yamauchi. If you forcibly asked her, it wouldn’t go anywhere. It’d be pointless.”
[says] (p.104) “Yeah. And I’m sorry. But I know for sure that she doesn’t hate you,” I answered.
[says] (p.104) “Did you know that Sakura loves digital cameras? I’ve heard that her camera doesn’t work, and she doesn’t have enough points to buy a new one. But what if you got one for her, Yamauchi? If you gave it to her as a present?” I asked.
[says] (p.104) “Well, there a few options in this special test. If you were the VIP and managed to make it through without being found out, or if you became a traitor and sold out your group, or if you managed to guide your group to clear the test, you’d get enough points to buy tons of digital cameras. Right?”
[says] (p.105) “Now, Yamauchi Haruki, you need to achieve real results. Work hard, and show Sakura how manly you are. Only then would you be a guy who deserves to go out with a former idol.”
[says] (p.105) “That’s right, Yamauchi. You can definitely do it.”
[trait] (p.105) Redirects the rage into the test as “a temporary fix”; worries Yamauchi might get too fired up and miss the VIP, then deliberately withholds the caution — a missed target is fine, the bigger picture matters more.
[rel:yamauchi-haruki] (pp.103–105) The mark, the lie, and the stimulus.
[rel:sakura-airi] (pp.104–105) Her camera and her idol past become the bait; her contact stays protected.

[says] (p.117) “That voice… Kushida?”

[says] (p.213) “Oh, no. Looks like you slept well.”
[says] (p.213) “I should be asking you the same thing. When did you get here?”
[says] (p.213) “Any results?” I asked.
[says] (p.213) “Oh no, it’s not a bother. I don’t mind.”
[says] (p.213) “Well, yeah. Of course I do. I’ve thought about getting up to Class A. No, wait…I guess it’d probably be more accurate to say that I must aim for Class A.”
[says] (p.214) “Nowadays, you can’t just enter college or get a job that easily. Jobs, especially,” I said.
[says] (p.214-215) “But this school… Well, it does have an incredible amount of influence, right? Most people who get anywhere in life succeed because … someone influential helps them. Or are you not interested in that, Ichinose?”
[says] (p.215) “Be that as it may, whether or not you can really earn twenty million points is another problem. Even if you score well on the exams and save your points, it probably wouldn’t be enough,” I said.
[says] (p.215) “Yeah. Class D’s financial situation is especially awful. Even though Horikita’s trying her hardest, the points from the island test have yet to be deposited. Actually, it’s entirely possible we’ll lose them in this test,” I said.
[says] (p.216) “Sorry. When you were using your phone earlier, I just happened to look at the screen. I was a little curious, so I was thinking of asking you about it.”
[says] (p.216) “I don’t plan on telling anyone else. Besides, I might’ve been mistaken. I won’t pry.”
[says] (p.216) “Have you found a way to win this thing?” I asked.
[says] (p.217) “To be honest, I have no idea. We haven’t really been able to talk, which means we haven’t been able to engage,” I said. (official answer)
[feat] (p.217) The phone-swap trap: Kushida–Horikita switch so a traitor submitting Horikita loses — Outcome #1 for Class D.
[says] (p.219) “You’re serious about this, huh? Well, if you’re all going to bet on that, I think I’ll join in, too,” I said.
[says] (p.220) “Sure. After hearing Hamaguchi out, I honestly don’t think we have another option. I’m really bad at communicating, so all I can do is show you the truth,” I answered.
[says] (p.220) “Wait, Ayanokouji. There’s no way this kind of strategy is going to work,” said Yukimura. (his line)
[trait] (p.221) The dam image — his email-show opens the hole so the muddy stream can run.
[rel:sotomura-hideo] (p.217) The Professor’s “All alone with Ichinose-dono?” barrages with Yukimura on entry.
[says] (p.223) “Yukimura isn’t the VIP. I heard as much earlier,” I said.
[trait] (p.223) Denying further would invite suspicion — stops because Yukimura is the swapped VIP.
[rel:yukimura-teruhiko] (pp.223-224) The withheld truth: he exchanged phones in secret; Class D’s 500,000 was already gambled on the swap.

### y1v4 p.225–243 — Ch.5 §5.2–5.3: found out, then the café
[scene] (p.225) The password-show settles Yukimura’s cover in the room; Ichinose’s call and SIM-lock lecture strip the double swap bare; then the 11 p.m. café with Karuizawa, Hirata, Horikita, Sudou and Ryuuen, the twelve-group results, and a warning over Class C’s lead.
[trait] (p.225) Names the real weakness of the password theatre: judging whose phone it was stayed hard, but the six-digit unlock “wasn’t the result of deduction, but rather of preconceived notions.”
[says] (p.225) “I’m sorry, Yukimura-kun. This happened because I came up with this idea at the very last minute,” I muttered.
[rel:yukimura-teruhiko] (p.225) Takes the fall as the points-grabber everyone now believes him to be.
[rel:ichinose-honami] (pp.225-233) The long reveal chain: her call, the SIM lecture, the Hoshinomiya-sensei unlock, the Karuizawa guess, and the mutual “we’re even.”
[says] (p.226) “Yeah. Guess so,” I answered.
[says] (p.228) “Not especially. I can’t do anything but believe, anyway. I’m heading to my room.”
[says] (p.230) “Horikita, of course.”
[says] (p.230) “A huge success? Don’t you mean a crushing defeat? We failed, hard. You saw through everything.”
[says] (p.230) “I’m sorry. Sorry for trying to deceive you like that, especially after I agreed to be your ally. Are you angry?”
[says] (p.230) “I see. If you mean it, I’m sure Horikita will be relieved.”
[says] (p.230) “The critical part?”
[says] (p.232) “The same as you. I would’ve tried to find out who the VIP was, borrowed that person’s phone, and had another one ready. Then, I’d step forward and claim to be the VIP myself.”
[says] (p.232) “So I’ve been found out, huh?”
[says] (p.232) “Why did you think it was Karuizawa?” I asked.
[says] (p.232) “Why didn’t you say anything? At the very least, you could have exposed my lie,” I said.
[says] (p.233) “Machida?”
[says] (p.233) “You ought to praise Horikita. She just gave me directions, that’s all.”
[feat] (p.231) The full chain on the page: Hirata told him Karuizawa was the real VIP; Manabe’s bullying of Karuizawa opened the swap; he replicated email and call history, paid for a SIM lock release, transferred his number, then swapped that phone with Yukimura’s.
[trait] (p.231) The trap’s shape on the page: a simple person misses the swap, a clever person accuses him, “But they never would have come to the conclusion that Karuizawa was the real VIP.”
[rel:karuizawa-kei] (p.231) The real VIP and phone owner — the fact kept even from Yukimura until Ichinose named her.
[rel:hirata-yousuke] (pp.231, 234) Hirata’s withheld knowledge, then the café approach about “that piece of paper.”
[says] (p.234) “Don’t worry. I’m positive that one of the guys from Class A sent the school an email with my name,” I answered.
[says] (p.234) “Sorry for calling you so late.” (his line to Karuizawa)
[says] (p.235) “She’s the type to come at the very last second. We have another four minutes,” I said.
[says] (p.235) “You’re finally here. Hey, who’s that behind you?” I asked.
[rel:sudou-ken] (pp.235-241) The café fifth wheel; his “one step behind” on the email; Ryuuen’s arrival makes him half-threaten and then sink back on command.
[says] (p.236) “Yeah. Someone discovered his identity,” I reasoned. (Minami, Horse VIP)
[note] (p.236) “Minami-kun was the Horse group VIP” — two Minami rows exist in the roster; which one is not settled on this page.
[says] (p.236) “Is Yamauchi okay?” I asked.
[says] (p.236) “I don’t know who it was, but betraying the group before he could was a fine move,” I said.
[rel:ryuuen-kakeru] (pp.237-243) The planted sixth guest; the Kushida reveal; the Class A VIP list; the explicit next-target threat; Ayanokōji’s quiet “we might be in trouble.”
[says] (p.242) “I mean, it’s not really a difficult problem. If you just put your mind to it, it’s relatively simple,” I told everyone.
[says] (p.242) “Putting aside how Ryuuen found out the VIPs’ identities, all he had to do was tell everyone ‘Kushida is the VIP’ before the end of the test, right? Of course, no one would believe what someone like Ryuuen says. Especially a group of such intelligent, gifted people. However, the final half hour was different. Even if you answered incorrectly, there wasn’t any risk to class points. As such, even someone who’d been playing defensively like Katsuragi could vote, right? If there was even a 1% chance that Kushida was really the VIP, then they would’ve gotten Outcome #1, which was the most convenient for everyone involved.”
[rel:katsuragi-kouhei] (p.242) The defensive player who could still vote in the riskless half hour.
[rel:kouenji-rokusuke] (p.242) “Kouenji saved our asses” — his betrayal email kept Class C from taking everything; the stray bullets hit other VIPs.
[says] (p.243) “Horikita. I think that we might be in trouble,” I said.
[says] (p.243) “You’ve got a point. I’m probably overthinking things. Don’t worry.”
[trait] (p.243) The premonition named as “something like excitement” — first stirrings of wanting Ryuuen as an opponent.
[rel:horikita-suzune] (pp.235-243) The café debrief, the results, and the shared worry she talks him out of.

### y1v4 p.244 — Ch.6: the author’s forward look
[scene] (p.244) The closing note turns the page on him by name.
[note] (p.244) The second semester is about to begin; it’s possible that a certain figure from Ayanokouji Kiyotaka’s past will make an appearance in Volume 5 — and it won’t just involve his classmates, the upperclassmen will get involved too.

## y1v4.5

<!-- walk: y1v4.5 → start -->

### y1v4.5 p.10 — Ch.1: the collection frame
[scene] (p.10) One-page first-person frame before the vignettes: no character appears and no school business is named — only the narrator’s case against end-of-summer melancholy.
[narrates] (p.10) “This is a selection of vignettes about students wavering in that space between childhood and adulthood.”
[trait] (p.10) The frame’s stance: refuses the end-of-vacation blues — the free years of life are the school years, and most of a life is spent sacrificing for society.
[note] (p.10) The page prints no name; read as the series narrator’s frame, following the first volume’s opening monologue.

<!-- walk: y1v4.5 ch1 → p.10 -->

### y1v4.5 p.11–17 — Ch.2: the fortune-teller invite chain
[scene] (p.11) Between the island and cruise tests he spends one of seven rest days alone in the dorm — no calls in either direction — until Sudou rings about a Keyaki Mall fortune-teller.
[status:points] (p.11) A point check before the call: 106,219 on hand, 100,000 transferred to Sudou Ken; the page gives no reason for the move.
[says] (p.11) “Well, I don’t really care,” I said aloud.
[says:sudou-ken] (p.13) “Definitely invite Suzune out, too.”
[rel:sudou-ken] (p.13–14) The outing is a device to reach Horikita — Sudou routes the invitation through him and will not take no for an answer.
[says] (p.16) “In other words, you’re talking about fortune-tellers’ ability to cold read, right?” I asked.
[trait] (p.16) Treats her claim of real power as a mechanism question — pushes until the explanation is on the table: fortune-tellers work from past data, not force.
[says:horikita-suzune] (p.17) “I refuse,” she answered.
[rel:horikita-suzune] (p.15–17) She talks him through cold reading, then declines the crowded outing; Sudou’s reply to the report is “Then forget about it.”

### y1v4.5 p.18–27 — Ch.2 §2.1: the couples-only floor
[scene] (p.18) At 9:30 the next morning the fifth floor is already a wall of couples; the booth’s rule turns him away and puts Ibuki Mio in his path.
[action] (p.18) Crowd reflex on display: alone in an elevator he would hammer the close button, so he detours across the building for an empty car.
[rule] (p.19–20) The written notice and the attendant agree: guidance is for couples only — Ukon-sensei reads fortunes for couples.
[rel:ibuki-mio] (p.24–26) He reads her anxiety as hyper-awareness — the difference is awareness — and tests it with the convenience-store clerk; she concedes the point, then compares him to a future mass murderer.
[says] (p.25) “The difference between you now, and you then, is awareness.”
[trait] (p.26) His loner’s answer to the jibe: “More or less.” — the comparison lands without flinching.

### y1v4.5 p.28–29 — Ch.2 §2.2–2.3: no partner, then fate
[scene] (p.28) Back in the dorm he researches tenchuusatsu and rejects every possible partner, only to meet Ibuki at the booth again the next day.
[note] (p.28) The list of refusals: Horikita would decline, he lacks the courage to ask Kushida, Sakura would be ill at ease among couples, and the guys would not spend their last summer days on a paired reading.
[says] (p.28) “I wonder if tenchuusatsu is only being offered this month?” I muttered.
[rel:ibuki-mio] (p.29) The second meeting turns from the booth’s rule to belief itself: he asks directly whether she believes, and she answers that the over-skeptical get nothing out of it.

### y1v4.5 p.30–32 — Ch.2 §2.3: the pair proposal
[scene] (p.30) Ibuki’s belief speech closes; he proposes they pair up for the couples-only booth — no relationship, no complications.
[says] (p.30) “In that case, why don’t we go together?”
[trait] (p.30) On his own feelings: “I honestly felt absolutely nothing for Ibuki.” — a flat line, first-time-customer ease.
[rel:ibuki-mio] (p.30–31) She worries the island grudge will stain him; he answers that the school runs on ability, not feelings, and that sabotage was a natural tactic under the circumstances.
[says] (p.31) “I don’t think people like that would be admitted to this school in the first place.”
[trait] (p.31) “All I am is an unremarkable student.” — no ladder to climb, no rung to be kicked off; coasting with Horikita is luck enough.

### y1v4.5 p.33–37 — Ch.2 §2.4: the reading
[scene] (p.33) After about fifteen minutes a pair at a time, the tent: thirty lux, hood, crystal ball, card reader — and the Basic Plan for both.
[says] (p.33) “I’ll take the same,” I replied.
[status:points] (p.34) Balance check before paying: roughly 6,000 points left — “barely enough” — and the Basic Plan deducts by card, price not printed.
[rel:ukon] (p.35–37) The reading turns on him: a broad “harsh childhood,” then the pronouncement that he holds the fate tenchuusatsu, and the advice to go straight home without detours.
[says] (p.38) “I’m not going to dismiss fortune-telling from now on,” I added.

### y1v4.5 p.38–47 — Ch.2 §2.5: the detour and the elevator
[scene] (p.38) They take the detour on purpose — to test the prediction — and the car grinds to a halt on the third floor.
[action] (p.39–40) Works the dead emergency line like a technician: backup battery alive, control panel likely dead, hearing-impaired button silent too.
[says] (p.40) “Apparently, our situation is far more serious than I originally thought.” — his own battery at four percent.
[says] (p.41) “A prank like this is evil,” I muttered. — the emergency number scribbled over with magic marker.
[action] (p.41) Picks the rescuer himself: the three idiots would make a scene, Sakura would be flustered, Kushida and Karuizawa unsuited — the taciturn fellow it is; the phone dies mid-call.
[trait] (p.45) Admits the express-mode push is theatre — he does not expect it to work; he is buying time and handing Ibuki hope while she is on the verge of losing her cool.
[says] (p.47) “Why don’t you try pushing the button for your desired floor, plus the close-door button at the same time?” I suggested.
[note] (p.47) The car moves — but the adults had used the remote from the first floor at the exact same moment; the pair’s button work did nothing.

### y1v4.5 p.48 — Ch.2: Katsuragi at the doors
[scene] (p.48) Outside stand two adults and Katsuragi, who took the call and moved the elevator himself.
[rel:katsuragi-kouhei] (p.48) The thanks run both ways: Sudou “performed brilliantly,” cooperation across classes is praised, and Ayanokōji owes him his nerve.
[back] (p.48) The friendship and the plan are treated as already told — “The story of our friendship had begun a little while ago.”

[scene] (p.49) One week before the elevator, Kushida's group-chat message asks the boys to celebrate Inogashira's birthday; AK notes the class is broke until the September 1 deposit.
[says:kushida-kikyou] (p.49) “Next Wednesday is Inogashira-san’s birthday.”
[trait] (p.49) Inogashira is “a somewhat quiet, plain girl from Class D, rather like Sakura” — AK's comparison for the reader.
[inference] (p.49) Ike jumps at the invitation because he likes Kushida and wants to be in her good graces; AK reads the event as his chance to get closer to her.
[says:ike-kanji] (p.49) “Let’s get some presents for Kokoro-chan!”
[says:yamauchi-haruki] (p.49) “Well, I don’t got any money.”
[rule] (p.49) The class's promised special-exam points will not be deposited until September 1; AK has almost nothing left after the fortune-teller.
[says] (p.50) “Isn’t it all right if the guys just pool their points and buy her one present?”
[rule] (p.50) “We had been awarded 8,700 private points at the start of August.”
[rule] (p.50) Free meals and free water make going without spending possible, but most students run dry near month's end, as with the first month's 100,000 points.
[action] (p.50) All three agree to the pooled gift and set out to buy it together.

### y1v5 p.202–204 — Ch.9 §9.4: the White Room greeting

### y1v6 p.14–16 — Ch.2 §2: student council handover
[scene] (p.14) Mid-October: student council election ceremony; Horikita Manabu hands over and Nagumo Miyabi is sworn in as president.
[says:nagumo-miyabi] (p.14) “Hello. I’m Nagumo, second-year Class A. Student Council President Horikita, I sincerely appreciate the strict yet kind guidance you’ve shown me.”
[trait] (p.14) The younger Horikita no longer shrinks from her brother; she remains steadfast watching him leave the stage.
[says:nagumo-miyabi] (p.15) “Allow me to introduce myself again. My name is Nagumo Miyabi, and I will be assuming the position of student council president at the Tokyo Metropolitan Advanced Nurturing High School. I sincerely look forward to working with all of you.”
[rule] (p.15) Nagumo pledges indefinite terms for student-council officers, annuls the October general election, lifts size limits, and allows majority-vote removal.
[says:nagumo-miyabi] (p.15) “To bring this school into the future, I intend to thoroughly destroy the past.”
[says:nagumo-miyabi] (p.16) “A revolution is coming. Students with real ability will rise to the top, and students without any will fall.”
[narrates] (p.16) Second-years shout delight at the announcement; first years do not know the second/third-year tension behind it.

### y1v6 p.17–19 — Ch.2 §2.1: changed classmates and Satou’s approach
[scene] (p.17) Second semester: Class D is coalescing; Ayanokouji finds himself alone when Ike, Yamauchi and the Professor leave for Keyaki Mall.

[rel:satou-maya] (p.18) Satou waits for him alone and asks to talk elsewhere.

[rel:satou-maya] (p.19) In the gym passage Satou asks whether he has a girlfriend and proposes exchanging phone numbers as friends.

[says:satou-maya] (p.21) “To be honest, after getting a good look at you, you’re even cooler than Hirata-kun, and more mature and gentler, too.”
[rel:sakura-airi] (p.22) Recalling Yamauchi’s earlier confession to Sakura puts his own situation in perspective.
[rel:satou-maya] (p.21) He feels nothing for Satou either way and notes she never actually asked him to go out.

### y1v7 p.65–97 — Ch.3 §3.6–3.8: chairman, Chabashira unmasked, Karuizawa cut off, Manabu’s bridge
[scene] (p.65) Finishes the office fight with his father, survives Chairman Sakayanagi’s admission revelations, unmasks Chabashira on the walk out, ends the Karuizawa pipeline by phone, then takes Manabu’s number over tea.
[says] (p.65) “This is the last thing I’ll say to you, Kiyotaka. Consider very carefully before answering me. Which would you prefer? To run away from this school of your own free will, or to have your parent force you to leave?”
[says] (p.65) “I don’t know if there’s any help for a man like you, but I have no intention of giving up. This school is developing its students’ talents, even if it goes about it differently from you. I expect to learn a lot here,”

[says] (p.70) “It’s pointless to probe me so clumsily. I understand everything,”

[says] (p.82) “Of course, I’ll help you if you ever need me to. I made a promise, and I’ll honor it. You can contact me if there’s an emergency, but please delete all traces of our conversations. I already deleted your contact information.”
[says] (p.84) “A clean room’s such a great thing,”
[says] (p.88) “I’d like time to think about putting your little sister on the student council and manipulating her behind the scenes.”
[says] (p.89) “If I decide to cooperate, I’ll contact you,”
[says] (p.91) “I know, I know. I’m not gonna wimp out after suggesting we all do it again,”

### y1v7 p.154–156, 166, 174 — Ch.6: the emergency address and the English door
[scene] (p.154) Walks with the mall group while the rooftop crisis runs at two; leaves them to meet Chabashira, then climbs to the roof.
[note] (p.154) Received Karuizawa’s emergency text that morning; does not reply, knowing Ryuuen’s only goal was to lure him.
[says] (p.154) “Nothing’s going on,”
[says] (p.154) “Nothing’s up. You were about to ask something, that’s all.”
[says] (p.154) “She just invited me to hang out with her, that’s all.”
[says] (p.154) “I don’t know how to answer that…”
[says] (p.155) “I’m happy to stay here, Airi. But I get why people want to go off campus,”
[says] (p.156) “Sorry,”
[says] (p.156) “I’m heading back.”
[says] (p.156) “To tell you the truth, I pulled an all-nighter, and I’m kind of beat. Let’s hang out again over the winter break,”
[says] (p.156) “Hello. I need to talk to you about something. Do you have a moment?”
[says] (p.156) “Yes, but there’re still a few loose ends that need tying up. I’d like to discuss this in person. Can we meet at school?”
[says] (p.156) “Understood. I’ll be there in a few minutes.”
[says] (p.156) “I was just wondering. Why do you want to make it to Class A so badly that you’d go so far as to use me to do it?”
[says] (p.157) “I can’t imagine that’s all there is to it. If you really aimed for Class A from the very beginning, you’d never have put Class D at a disadvantage the way you did.”
[says] (p.157) “You hesitated, right? You weren’t sure this class really had the ability to aim for Class A. You weren’t sure it was even worth trying.”
[says] (p.157) “If you won’t answer, then you might as well give up on trying to use me.”
[says] (p.157) “This is crucial. If you don’t take action now, Class D will never make it to Class A. If anything, it’s unlikely we’ll even reach Class C.”
[says] (p.157) “It’s already two p.m. Ryuuen is putting on a very interesting show right about now, after calling Karuizawa to the roof.”
[says] (p.157) “So, even you don’t know, Sensei? Karuizawa was horribly bullied in the past.”
[says] (p.157) “This is the first I’m hearing of it.”
[says] (p.157) “That information will probably be all over the school tomorrow. When that happens, Karuizawa will retreat into her shell, and maybe even voluntarily drop out of school. If we can prove Class C was responsible, we might be able to retaliate, but the damage done to each class will be unfathomable,”
[says] (p.158) “So, you’ll help me?”
[says] (p.158) “Naturally.”
[says] (p.158) “Can you really afford to turn me down?”
[says] (p.158) “There’s no guarantee that I won’t do anything to sabotage Class D in the future, is there? I’m more than capable of ensuring we never rise through the ranks.”
[says] (p.158) “If you do this for me, I promise I’ll do nothing to hinder or sabotage Class D going forward. I think that’s significant, don’t you?”
[says] (p.158) “Relax,”
[says] (p.158) “I had no intention of recruiting your assistance.”
[says] (p.158) “I was just testing you, that’s all. So, why not come observe this little drama’s conclusion?”
[says] (p.159) “What are you planning, Ayanokouji? How long do you intend to wait?”
[note] (p.159) Chabashira’s line; his answer is below.
[says] (p.159) “Let’s chat,”
[says] (p.159) “I’d like to discuss something that happened shortly after we started school, back when we wanted you to sell us one point for Sudou’s test.”
[says] (p.159) “There’s nothing you cannot buy with private points. That’s what you said, right?”
[says] (p.160) “If purchasing points was permissible, there would never be any expulsions, would there? Any time someone got a failing grade, someone else could cover for them the way we did.”
[says] (p.160) “True. But that’s a flaw in the system, isn’t it? If salvation through private points is always an option, it dramatically lowers the danger of expulsion based on test performance,”
[says] (p.160) “Back when I asked you to sell us that single point, you added a value to it, Chabashira-sensei.”
[says] (p.160) “No, that’s not what I mean. I’m asking whether exchanging one test point for one hundred thousand private points was something you came up with on the spot, or if you had some basis for attaching that price to it. At the time, I thought you sounded like you came up with it off the top of your head, but since then, I’ve come to doubt you arbitrarily decided that on your own without consulting the school.”
[says] (p.160) “This school has taken great care to legislate, in writing, all matters related to points, hasn’t it? It’s not far-fetched to think that it might’ve anticipated someone trying to purchase a test score increase. I would believe it.”
[says] (p.160) “That’s right. If you can answer, please do.”
[says] (p.161) “Should I interpret that to mean you can’t answer?”
[says] (p.161) “Very well. I’ll happily draw my own conclusions. The school prepared a manual to consult in every conceivable situation. It was determined in advance that it cost one hundred thousand private points to purchase one test point. If we go from there, it begs the question: can we can purchase one test point for a hundred thousand private points on more than one occasion?”
[says] (p.161) “Is the hundred thousand points per test point rate only available for a limited time after we’ve enrolled? Does the price go up every time we make a purchase? Or are we only allowed to make such a purchase once? The more I think about it, the more questions I have. Please tell me if I’m correct.”
[says] (p.161) “There is. I just need to ask you directly, Sensei.”
[says] (p.161) “How many points would I need to buy one test point on the next midterm right now?”
[says] (p.161) “As a teacher, you’re obligated to answer that question, right? If you won’t, I’ll go ahead and ask another teacher. And if they answer me, I’ll report to the school that Class D’s homeroom teacher is discriminating against us. Don’t forget that I can do that,”
[says] (p.162) “A few students are already doing just that, aren’t they? Look at Ichinose and Ryuuen, both accumulating private points.”
[note] (p.162) Chabashira’s line; the answer that follows is hers.
[says] (p.162) “So, you truly can’t answer my question?”
[says] (p.162) “Not at all. I was just having a friendly chat,”
[says] (p.162) “I suppose it’s time, wouldn’t you say? Time for this game to be over.”
[says] (p.163) “I’m heading to the roof,”
[says] (p.163) “It doesn’t matter. Even if Ryuuen learns that I orchestrated all Class D’s strategies so far, he now gains nothing. If anything, he might assume I’m involved next time, and end up sabotaging himself.”
[says] (p.163) “I see. Well, you were certainly correct that it’s easier for people to become attached to something if they have a goal to strive for. But you needn’t worry. I choose to stay here. I have no intention of going back to being under that man’s control.”
[says] (p.163) “It’s not a pipe dream. Class D is about to rise to Class C. Soon, Horikita will unite our class. I’m sure of it,”
[says] (p.164) “Such skepticism is unbecoming of a teacher. Horikita is more than capable of leading Class D.”
[says] (p.164) “Ultimately,”
[says] (p.164) “Horikita is starting to mature. The same goes for many of our classmates. Continue to guide them as their teacher, and our position as Class C will be secure…and so might our path to Class A.”
[says] (p.164) “Yes.”
[says] (p.164) “I can’t guarantee it. At most, I can say I’ll deal with the situation based on my evaluation of Ryuuen’s personality and behavior.”
[says] (p.164) “Well then, thank you very much for accompanying me this far.”
[says] (p.165) “He’s here as a witness. After all, Ryuuen will try to win by any means necessary, and I’d like to avoid this becoming violent.”
[says] (p.165) “Does the former student council president look like someone who’d ever do that sort of thing?”
[says] (p.165) “There will be a person on the rooftop who will witness what happens there. As long as that holds true, then we’ll be fine,”
[says] (p.165) “A few minutes after I head up, I’d like you to stop halfway up the stairs. There’s no need for you to talk to the students who come back down from the roof, nor is it necessary to punish them. Just make sure they see you on their way down. I need them to be aware you were here.”
[says] (p.165) “Of course. If I go back on my word, you may choose to forget what happens here today,”
[says] (p.165) “Hmm. Who knows?”
[says] (p.165) “I’ll be back in ten or twenty minutes.”
[says] (p.166) “May I pass?”
[says] (p.166) “Don’t panic. I’m the one you want,”
[says] (p.166) “I’m ending this today. No one else will interfere,”
[says] (p.166) “I intend to crush Ryuuen. He doesn’t stand a chance without your help,”
[says] (p.167) “Sorry I’m late,”
[says] (p.167) “What do you mean, ‘why’? I made a promise, didn’t I? I promised that I’d save you.”
[says] (p.167) “Karuizawa emailed me. She said she wanted me to save her.”
[says] (p.170) “It’s precisely because I was the most suspicious that I did things that way. Or perhaps it was because I had no other method available to me other than using Horikita?”
[says] (p.170) “I’m the person you’ve been searching for.”
[says] (p.171) “Am I in a dilemma?”
[says] (p.171) “I only came here because Karuizawa asked for help. If you want proof that I’m X, you could just wait until the next exam and see what I do,”
[says] (p.171) “Awful things?”
[says] (p.171) “What I’ll do? There’s nothing I can do.”
[says] (p.172) “I’ve already admitted to it. But if you won’t believe me, then allow me to explain, Ibuki,”
[says] (p.172) “The island exam. You were instructed to take pictures of our leader’s keycard, but your digital camera got damaged somehow. Am I wrong?”
[says] (p.172) “It was me. I used water to short-circuit the camera without causing visible damage. When I met you in the forest, Ibuki, your fingers were covered in dirt. There were signs that you were digging nearby. When I returned to the spot later that night, I found a wireless transceiver. That was so you could communicate with Ryuuen, right?”
[says] (p.172) “Trick?”
[says] (p.172–173) “There’s no trick I can pull in a situation like this. You already know Karuizawa’s big secret. … You took plenty of precautions to corner me. Right?”
[says] (p.173) “Indeed. I can’t exactly report what you just did to Karuizawa to the school, either,”
[says] (p.173) “Okay. Well, are you satisfied? If so, I’ll be taking Karuizawa back with me,”
[says] (p.173) “Sorry, Ryuuen. I can’t fulfil your expectations.”
[says] (p.173) “I was just dancing in the palm of your hand. That’s all.”
[says] (p.174) “I may have revealed myself to you, but that doesn’t mean I’m in trouble. Only Horikita and Karuizawa from Class D know who I am, so if this information makes its way to other classes, I’ll know one of you leaked it.”
[says] (p.174) “If you spread word of my actions, I’ll report everything that happened here to the school,”
[says] (p.174) “I can. All I have to do is sacrifice Karuizawa.”
[says] (p.174) “You assumed from the start that I’d abandon Karuizawa. But when I showed up here, you started talking as though you thought I wasn’t going to. Am I wrong?”
[says] (p.174) “You heard her. Believe what you like, but if you spill the beans about me to the other classes, it’ll be war,”

[says] (p.190) “Yeah, I suppose you’re right. So why didn’t you sell me out to Ryuuen?”
[says] (p.190) “Don’t think about it anymore. What happened today, what’s happened so far. You can think about that stuff later. The only thing that’s for certain right now, in this moment, is that your curse has been lifted. Manabe…no, no one will dig up your past again. As for what’s to come, well, you can just keep going the way you always have,”
[says] (p.190) “You’ve managed to overcome your past and create an identity as the person you are now,”
[says] (p.190) “Starting tomorrow, you can just pick up where you left off.”
[says] (p.191) “I hurt you. I won’t ask you to forgive me. But please remember just one thing. If something like what happened today happens again, I will come save you.”
[says] (p.191) “A few steps below us, the student council president—well, the former student council president—and Chabashira-sensei are waiting. They know the circumstances to a certain degree, so they should be able to handle things, including taking care of your soaked uniform,”
[says] (p.191) “I’ve still got a few things to tidy up here. Besides, we don’t want to be seen together. It’s best if you go first.”
[says] (p.192) “Do you think this…is over, Ayanokouji?”
[note] (p.192) Ryuuen’s line.
[says] (p.192) “It’s over,”
[says] (p.192) “Surely you’re not about to say you want to keep going?”
[says] (p.192) “Are you going to report me to the school?”
[says] (p.192) “Let me give you some advice. I don’t recommend it. The former student council president is waiting on the stairs as we speak. He may not know all the details of what happened here, but it’s obvious you were the one setting a trap, Ryuuen…especially since you were caught on video spraypainting the surveillance camera. I, on the other hand, was at Keyaki Mall when you did that. I can produce many alibis as I need to prove it.”
[says] (p.192) “Yes. I knew you wouldn’t stop attacking me until I struck you at least once.”
[says] (p.192) “Yeah, I do. There’s a reason I defeated you, Ryuuen. You made a mistake choosing the order of your foes. If you’d cut your teeth fighting Ichinose, and then Katsuragi and Sakayanagi, you might’ve been closer to my level by the time you chose to challenge me. But your curiosity got the better of you. You overplayed your hand.”
[says] (p.192–193) “I’d like to say I’d be happy to have a rematch, but I’m done drawing … Target someone else,”
[says] (p.193) “I’d like to avoid that, but yes.”
[says] (p.193) “Your overconfidence in relying on my identity and Karuizawa’s past was your undoing. If you’d been less reckless, you would’ve either mounted a larger-scale attack or posted more lookouts,”
[says] (p.193) “Not necessarily. As long as you make no further attempts to attack us, I won’t use this incident to advance myself.”
[says] (p.193) “Maybe,”
[says] (p.193) “But what are you going to do? You can’t undo what happened, Ryuuen.”
[says] (p.194) “Oh? That sounds serious. Taking responsibility and all.”
[says] (p.194) “Because you hate me. Divide the remaining points among everyone. Once the school expels me, I bet Katsuragi and Sakayanagi will void our contract, but there’s nothing to be done about that,”
[note] (p.194) Ryuuen’s line to Ibuki.
[says] (p.194) “Are you sure? I think you’ll come to regret this,”
[says] (p.195) “Why do you care?”
[note] (p.195) Ryuuen’s line.
[says] (p.195) “If you leave without knowing why you lost, you won’t grow,”
[says] (p.195) “Are you really fine with not knowing why you lost to me?”
[says] (p.195) “Let it go. There’s no point trying to save me. You’ve got nothing to gain by sparing me now that I know about you and Karuizawa. I could expose you at any time.”
[note] (p.195) Ryuuen’s line.
[says] (p.195) “True enough. If I must name a reason for sparing you…it’ll work to Class D’s advantage to have you crush Sakayanagi and Ichinose for us. Besides, if your contract with Katsuragi remains in place, Class A will continue to hemorrhage points. Most importantly, though, if you suddenly drop out of school, Sakayanagi and Ichinose will assume that X beat you. That spells trouble for me down the line,”
[says] (p.195) “So, trust me, this is to my benefit. Even if word of what happened up here gets out, I took no noticeable injuries. It would just look like you guys fought among yourselves, wouldn’t it?”
[says] (p.195) “Allow me to add just one thing,”
[says] (p.195) “You are free to drop out of school of your own volition and you’re free to doubt me. But I have absolutely no intention of telling anyone about today unless you force my hand. The former student council president has similarly promised to keep it to himself. Nothing about this situation is forcing you to withdraw—but if you do, I won’t stop you.”

[scene] (p.11) Third-semester road trip; Ayanokōji rides the Class C bus in a school-mandated jersey and listens to Chabashira’s Mixed Training Camp briefing while Ike talks across the aisle.
[says:chabashira-sae] (p.22) “Yes. The penalty is expulsion.”
[thinks] (p.22) Overall ranking uses the large group’s average, but expulsions turn on each small group’s average under the school’s threshold — “That was troubling.”
[thinks] (p.24) On the solidarity rule: a representative cannot casually drag an enemy class down unless someone was already “on the verge of dropping out anyway,” and peers would know who that was.
[thinks] (p.24) Texts a certain individual to learn whether the student council helped design this exam — no reply is recorded on these pages.
[rule] (p.25) Class penalty for an expulsion is 100 class points; a class without enough points floors at zero until the debt is paid.

[rel:nagumo-miyabi] (p.32) Plans to keep an eye on Nagumo during the rare mixed-grade exam.

### y1v9 p.24 — Name raised at the end of the council visit
[scene] (p.24) As the Sakayanagi–Nagumo audience ends, Nagumo asks about Ayanokōji.
[rel:nagumo-miyabi] (p.24) Nagumo raises the name; Sakayanagi says she is only somewhat familiar with it; Nagumo drops the topic and refuses her offer to investigate.
[says:sakayanagi-arisu] (p.24) “Ayanokōji…? I am somewhat familiar with that name. He’s a 1st year, is he not?”
[note] (p.24) Ayanokōji does not appear in person; only named in the closing exchange.
